===================================================================================
2. IMPORTANT CORRECTION — NATIVE RNG MAKES OLD 7-10 DESIGN NON-EXACT
===================================================================================
A deeper audit found the code immediately after the clamp:

  0x0560A648  ADD W1,W21,#1
  0x0560A64C  MOV W0,W21
  0x0560A658  BL  0x057193C8
  0x0560A660  STR W0,[X25,#0x20]

So RunUnit does NOT directly store W21.
It calls the native integer RNG with:
  min = W21
  max = W21 + 1

Independent call-site audit of 0x057193C8 confirms the upper bound is inclusive.
Example native call:
  min=0, max=2
followed by separate code paths for result 0, result 1, and result 2.

Therefore final stored monthly budget is:
  W21 OR W21+1

Consequence for the previous report:
  old pre-RNG range 7..10 could actually produce 7..11.

This is the main correction found in the re-audit.

===================================================================================
3. CORRECT EXACT 15-20 DESIGN
===================================================================================
User target now:
  Player-Sect NPC monthly Goal budget/opportunities = 15..20
  realm-gap penalty reduced, not removed
  non-Player-Sect NPC remains vanilla

Safest exact design while preserving the native RNG:

  if NOT Player-Sect NPC:
      W8 = vanilla SDIV penalty
      leave W21/base logic vanilla

  if Player-Sect NPC:
      nativePenalty  = floor(abs(realmGap)/npcGoalSub)
      reducedPenalty = floor(nativePenalty/2)
      reducedPenalty = min(reducedPenalty, 4)
      baseGoal        = 19

      pre-RNG W21 = 19 - reducedPenalty
      => W21 = 15..19

  native RunUnit then executes:
      RandomIntInclusive(W21, W21+1)
      => final stored budget = 15..20

Range table:
  reducedPenalty 0 -> pre-RNG 19 -> final 19/20
  reducedPenalty 1 -> pre-RNG 18 -> final 18/19
  reducedPenalty 2 -> pre-RNG 17 -> final 17/18
  reducedPenalty 3 -> pre-RNG 16 -> final 16/17
  reducedPenalty 4 -> pre-RNG 15 -> final 15/16

Overall union is exactly 15..20.

DO NOT use base=20 with cap=5 at this hook unless the downstream RNG is also changed;
that combination can produce 21.

===================================================================================
4. IMPORTANT SEMANTIC LIMIT — BUDGET IS NOT GUARANTEED COMPLETED ACTIONS
===================================================================================
The value stored at [X25+0x20] is best treated as the monthly Goal budget/opportunity
count used by RunUnit.

The later function still performs native candidate/category selection, prerequisite checks,
weighting, cooldowns, and Goal execution logic.

Therefore:
  15..20 = scheduler budget/opportunities
NOT:
  guaranteed 15..20 successful actions every month.

Actual completed progression actions can be lower when candidates fail native conditions.
This distinction is important for runtime diagnosis.

===================================================================================
5. npcGoalMaxWeightCount — DEEP CALLEE AUDIT CONFIRMS LEAVE VANILLA
===================================================================================
Getter:
  0x032F9F78

Use:
  0x0560C970 -> converted value stored in W24
  0x0560C99C -> W1 = W24
  0x0560C9AC -> BL 0x06C82BBC

Callee 0x06C82BBC was disassembled deeper.
It:
  - queries list/count
  - compares count against W2 + W1
  - when count is too large, computes excess
  - removes/trims entries until the allowed list size is reached

This confirms npcGoalMaxWeightCount behaves as a candidate/list limiter,
not the primary monthly RunUnit budget stored at [X25+0x20].

Recommendation for first 15-20 build:
  KEEP npcGoalMaxWeightCount VANILLA.

If runtime later shows 15-20 budget but poor Goal diversity, audit this separately.

===================================================================================
6. ACTION1031 CURRENT HOOK — VERIFIED
===================================================================================
WorldUnitAIAction1031.UpdateWeightAdd:

  Hook file 0x055ABA8C

Native context re-confirmed:
  0x055AFA40  get currentMonth -> W20
  0x055AFA44  LDR X8,[X19,#0x18]
  0x055AFA50  LDR X0,[X8,#0x18]
  0x055AFA5C  BL 0x05600B0C ; get_data
  0x055AFA64  LDR W22,[X0,#0x5C] ; lastTrainsMonst
  0x055AFA88  SUB W8,W20,W22
  0x055AFA8C  B 0x08C78680 ; current custom helper

Current custom helper @ 0x08C78680 still implements the old behavior:
  replay native MADD
  if currentMonth <= lastTrainsMonst -> native
  if requester is not Player-Sect -> native
  otherwise -> W8 = 60000

Thus the existing V60 build can continue giving cultivation VERY HIGH indefinitely
because it has no native EXP-ready stop-state check yet.

===================================================================================
7. fullExpMonth / Data+0x10 — STRONGLY RE-VALIDATED FROM NATIVE CODE
===================================================================================
Action1031 owner/data chain remains:
  X19 Action1031
  [X19+0x18] owner/context
  [owner+0x18] WorldUnitAIDataBase
  get_data @ 0x05600B0C
  returned Data +0x10 = state/month field previously mapped as fullExpMonth

Independent native progression path found around 0x0560E83C:

When native phase condition equals 1:
  0x0560E908  MOV X0,X19
  0x0560E90C  BL  get_data
  0x0560E914  STR WZR,[X0,#0x10]

So Data+0x10 is explicitly reset to zero by native phase handling.

Then native code:
  reads [Data+0x10]
  skips the readiness test if already nonzero
  when zero, calls native progression predicate @ 0x0405BB04
  if predicate succeeds, obtains current month
  0x0560E9AC  STR W0,[X20,#0x10]

Therefore Data+0x10 is demonstrably a native month-stamped progression state:
  zero     = readiness state not yet recorded
  nonzero  = native readiness state already recorded
  reset to zero on the appropriate new-phase transition

This is stronger binary evidence for using it as Action1031's stop signal.
No manual EXP, Realm, Grade, or breakthrough-state write is needed.

===================================================================================
8. ACTION1031 RECOMMENDED STATE-AWARE LOGIC
===================================================================================
Recommended helper:

  nativeWeight = original MADD result

  if currentMonth <= lastTrainsMonst:
      return nativeWeight

  if requester is not Player-Sect NPC:
      return nativeWeight

  aiDataBase = owner +0x18
  if aiDataBase is unavailable:
      return nativeWeight

  data = get_data(aiDataBase)
  if data is unavailable:
      return nativeWeight

  if data[+0x10] != 0:
      return nativeWeight

  return 60000

Meaning:
  - while native readiness state is zero, cultivation gets VERY HIGH 60000
  - after native readiness is recorded, cultivation falls back to vanilla weight
  - Goal141 / other eligible progression Goals can compete again
  - native timing and realm transition remain authoritative

This is still preferable to directly calling/reimplementing ConfRoleGrade.IsUpGrade
inside the helper or manually comparing/writing EXP.

0. BASELINE

===============================================================================
1. FINAL PROGRESSION STATE MODEL
===============================================================================
The best native state is NOT fullExpMonth alone.

Native progression helpers:
  ConfRoleGrade.GetGradeItemInExp  0x0405B110
  ConfRoleGrade.GetNextGradeItem   0x0405B1C8
  ConfRoleGrade.IsUpGrade          0x0405BB04
  ConfRoleGrade.IsPhaseStop        0x0405BCC4
  ConfRoleGrade.GetPhaseProps      0x0405BD80

PropertyData fields used by native progression:
  +0x48 = current EXP
  +0x4C = current gradeID

General cap-state check:

  next = ConfRoleGrade.GetNextGradeItem(currentGradeID)

  if next == null:
      UNKNOWN/END -> vanilla

  if currentExp < next.exp:
      NEED_EXP

  if currentExp >= next.exp:
      progression is CAPPED at the next transition threshold

Then current phase distinguishes:
  Early/Middle + capped -> MINOR_CAPPED
  Late + capped         -> MAJOR_CAPPED

This state is better than fullExpMonth for Action1031 because it works for all
Early/Middle/Late transitions and does not wait for the later System-phase timestamp.

===============================================================================
2. ACTION1031 — FINAL SMART CULTIVATION RULE
===============================================================================
WorldUnitAIAction1031.UpdateWeightAdd:
  hook VA   0x055AFA8C
  hook file 0x055ABA8C
  current bytes FD 22 DB 14
  current instruction branches to old helper 0x08C78680

Current old behavior:
  if Player-Sect and >=1 month since cultivation:
      final weight = 60000

Problem:
  Early/Middle NPC whose EXP is already capped still gets 60000 because
  fullExpMonth remains zero outside major-ready Late state.

Recommended final behavior:

  nativeWeight = original native calculation

  if not Player-Sect NPC:
      return nativeWeight

  next = GetNextGradeItem(currentGradeID)
  if next == null:
      return nativeWeight

  if currentExp >= next.exp:
      return nativeWeight

  if currentMonth <= lastTrainsMonst:
      return nativeWeight

  return 60000

Meaning:
  NEED_EXP      -> very high cultivation
  MINOR_CAPPED  -> stop forcing cultivation; allow buy/use phase item
  MAJOR_CAPPED  -> stop forcing cultivation; allow major breakthrough pipeline

This removes the known one-cycle fullExpMonth delay for the custom priority rule.

===============================================================================
3. GETPHASEPROPS — EXACT NEXT-PHASE ITEM IDS
===============================================================================
ConfRoleGrade.GetPhaseProps:
  RVA 0x0405BD80

Direct binary behavior:
  input includes WorldUnit-like requester
  requester +0x68 -> progression/dyn data chain
  obtains current gradeID
  calls GetNextGradeItem
  reads nextGradeItem +0xF0
  splits/parses the configured ID string/list
  returns the exact native phase-prop ID list

This means custom logic does NOT need hardcoded pill IDs.

Important xref result:
  direct GetPhaseProps callers are UI / progression-event paths.
  No direct call was found from ordinary Action1004, Action1029, or Goal142.

Thus the ordinary NPC AI does not appear to explicitly ask:
  "Which exact phase item do I need next?"

That is the missing intelligence we can add without replacing the native item system.

===============================================================================
4. PHASE ITEMS ARE REAL MARKET ITEMS
===============================================================================
The game's own visible data/text describes Early->Middle and Middle->Late
breakthrough items as items purchased at Markets for the relevant realm/area.

This agrees with binary behavior in Action1004 and UnitActionPropUse:
  acquire item -> use item -> native phase transition.

No direct RewardPropItem grant is needed for the smart-AI design.

===============================================================================
5. UNIT ACTION PROP USE — NATIVE PHASE VALIDATION
===============================================================================
UnitActionPropUse:
  ctor         0x05487D10
  alt ctor     0x05484E00
  IsCreate     0x05487F24
  OnCreate     0x05489A84

Special progression item-config branch includes type 106.
A second special category 653 also exists, but its human-readable enum/name
is not required for implementation.

For type 106, native IsCreate:
  gets current gradeID
  calls GetNextGradeItem
  reads current PropertyData EXP (+0x48)
  compares it against nextGradeItem.exp (+0x48)

If the EXP/phase condition does not match, the use action is rejected.

Therefore our AI should NOT bypass UnitActionPropUse.IsCreate.
It should only prioritize the correct already-valid candidate.

===============================================================================
6. PROPSDATA GETTERS — ACTION1029 UNKNOWN CLOSED
===============================================================================
Old matching metadata preserves the PropsData method sequence:

  get_propsType
  set_propsType
  get_propsID
  set_propsID
  get_propsCount
  set_propsCount
  ...

V60 binary bodies resolve the tiny getter wrappers as:

  0x0409E9E8 = PropsData.get_propsType
    -> generic value slot 0

  0x0409E9F0 = PropsData.get_propsID
    -> generic value slot 1

This is also independently consistent with Action1029:
  0x055AEE10 calls 0x0409E9E8 and compares result as a type/category
  0x055AEE48 / 0x055AEEC4 call 0x0409E9F0 before adding candidate

CRITICAL FINAL RESULT:
  Action1029 candidate List<int> stores direct PropsData.propsID.

Therefore:
  GetPhaseProps() returned IDs
can be compared DIRECTLY against
  Action1029 candidate List<int> values.

No soleID/inventory-key resolution layer is required for candidate comparison.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

===============================================================================
7. ACTION1029 — OWNED PHASE ITEM PRIORITY
===============================================================================
WorldUnitAIAction1029.ActionStart:
  0x055AEB18

Candidate construction:
  - enumerates NPC inventory
  - resolves PropsData
  - has TWO acceptance routes, not only one

Route A:
  item/config branch with type/category check 103

Route B:
  secondary config lookup at 0x0338D0D4
  accepts when secondary field +0x18 == 201

Thus the old simplistic statement "Action1029 only accepts type 103" was wrong.
There are two native acceptance paths.

For either accepted path, Action1029 obtains:
  PropsData.get_propsID @ 0x0409E9F0
and adds that propsID to the candidate List<int>.

Duplicate candidate IDs are prevented by the native list/contains helper around:
  0x07158774

Native randomization:
  VA   0x055AEF98
  file 0x055AAF98
  bytes 2E 5F 5B 94
  original BL 0x06C86C50
  high-confidence CommonTool.SortRandom<int>

Register/context at this hook:
  X20 = Action1029 this
  X21 = candidate List<int> containing direct propsID values
  X0  = X21 just before call
  X1  = 0
  X2  = generic method/context pointer loaded immediately before call

Requester chain:
  [X20+0x18] -> AI owner/context
  [owner+0x10] -> requester WorldUnit

After randomization, native loop:
  candidate = list[index]
  resolve owned PropsData
  construct UnitActionPropUse @ 0x05487D10
  requester.CreateAction via vtable +0x1C8

Result:
  CreateAction == 0 -> success -> Action1029 stops immediately
  CreateAction != 0 -> tries next candidate

SAFEST CUSTOM BEHAVIOR:

  call vanilla SortRandom first

  if not Player-Sect:
      leave list unchanged

  if progression state != MINOR_CAPPED:
      leave list unchanged

  phaseIDs = GetPhaseProps(requester)

  scan candidate List<int>
  if candidate propsID matches any phaseID:
      move/swap that candidate to index 0

  return to native loop

If exact phase item is invalid for any native reason, UnitActionPropUse/CreateAction
still rejects it and Action1029 continues to later vanilla candidates.

No forced use is needed.

===============================================================================
8. ACTION1004 — MARKET PHASE ITEM PRIORITY
===============================================================================
WorldUnitAIAction1004:
  CreateActionInit 0x0559E8F4
  ActionStart      0x0559FABC

Relevant action fields:
  +0x30 allBuyProps
  +0x38 buyBuild
  +0x40 buySystemName
  +0x48 buyPropsType

CreateActionInit produces a FINAL filtered candidate list after native checks
such as market/build/town conditions and item filters.

ActionStart buyPropsType==1 path:
  list = [Action1004+0x30]
  count = [list+0x18]

Random candidate selection hook:
  VA   0x0559FCC4
  file 0x0559BCC4
  bytes C1 E5 05 94
  original BL CommonTool.Random @ 0x057193C8

Context immediately before hook:
  X20 = Action1004 this
  W21 = allBuyProps count
  W0  = 0
  W1  = W21
  X2  = 0
  X3  = 0

Return:
  W0 = selected index
  copied to W23 immediately after call

Requester chain available from Action1004:
  [X20+0x18] -> owner/context
  [owner+0x10] -> requester WorldUnit

Selected list entry:
  entry +0x10 = propsID used by native ConfItemProps/item lookup

Special native handling proves type106 is a supported buy-and-use progression route:
  0x0559FDB4 compares selected item type with 106
  type 401 or 106 forces purchase count = 1

Native economy remains:
  CostPropItem   around 0x0559FE98
  RewardPropItem around 0x0559FEC4

After purchase:
  0x0559FFFC compares type with 106 again
  native retrieves the owned item
  constructs UnitActionPropUse around 0x055A0950
  calls requester.CreateAction via vtable +0x1C8

This proves Action1004 already has a native route:
  select type106 progression item
  -> PAY for it
  -> receive it
  -> immediately try native UnitActionPropUse

SAFEST CUSTOM BEHAVIOR:

  vanillaIndex = CommonTool.Random(0,count)

  if not Player-Sect:
      return vanillaIndex

  if progression state != MINOR_CAPPED:
      return vanillaIndex

  phaseIDs = GetPhaseProps(requester)

  scan FINAL allBuyProps list
  if an existing candidate entry.propsID matches any phaseID:
      return that existing candidate index

  return vanillaIndex

This does NOT:
  - add unavailable market stock
  - bypass town/build filters
  - bypass ItemPropsFilter
  - give a free item
  - bypass price/cost
  - bypass UnitActionPropUse validation

===============================================================================
9. DUPLICATE / ACTION GUARD — CUSTOM GUARD NOT REQUIRED
===============================================================================
For the candidate-only architecture, a separate custom duplicate-action guard is
NOT required.

Reason:

A) Action1029
  - only reorders an existing native candidate list
  - constructs the same native UnitActionPropUse as vanilla
  - CreateAction result 0 stops the loop immediately
  - failure tries the next candidate
  - no extra action is created beyond vanilla behavior

B) Action1004
  - only changes which EXISTING final candidate index is selected
  - native buy/economy/use path remains single-action logic
  - no extra market item or additional action is injected

C) Action1031
  - only changes final weight
  - does not create an extra action by itself

Therefore native CreateAction / active-action state remains the guard.

This is significantly safer than:
  - injecting an extra UnitActionPropUse
  - injecting a custom Goal
  - writing phase/grade directly

===============================================================================
10. ABI / REGISTER SAFETY AT THE THREE TARGET HOOKS
===============================================================================
A) Action1031 @ 0x055AFA8C
--------------------------------
Current hook is already a branch to custom code.
Future helper should:
  - reproduce the displaced native weight calculation as needed
  - preserve all native callee-saved state
  - use X19 as Action1031 this
  - resolve requester through owner/context
  - compute cap-state via current EXP + GetNextGradeItem
  - return final W8
  - branch back to 0x055AFA90

Because this is a B/trampoline, not a normal BL replacement, helper must explicitly
restore any temporary caller/native values it consumes before returning to the caller body.

B) Action1029 @ 0x055AEF98
--------------------------------
Original instruction is a normal BL SortRandom<int>.
Wrapper may emulate normal call-clobber behavior for caller-saved registers,
but MUST preserve AArch64 callee-saved X19-X29.

Critical live state:
  X20 = Action1029 this
  X21 = candidate List<int>

Safest wrapper order:
  1) call original SortRandom with original X0/X1/X2
  2) inspect X20/X21 while preserving callee-saved registers
  3) optionally swap matching propsID to list index 0
  4) return to 0x055AEF9C

C) Action1004 @ 0x0559FCC4
--------------------------------
Original instruction is a normal BL Random.
Wrapper MUST preserve X19-X29.

Critical live state:
  X20 = Action1004 this
  W21 = count

Safest wrapper order:
  1) call original Random(0,count) and save its returned index
  2) Player-Sect + MINOR_CAPPED check
  3) scan [X20+0x30] only
  4) if exact phase propsID exists, return its index in W0
  5) else restore vanilla random result in W0
  6) return to 0x0559FCC8

===============================================================================
11. WHY CANDIDATE-ONLY IS THE BEST FIRST PROTOTYPE
===============================================================================
The new AI does not manufacture progression.
It only improves decision quality at points where vanilla has already approved options.

Vanilla remains responsible for:
  - EXP threshold
  - phase/realm config
  - phase item IDs
  - inventory ownership
  - market stock
  - market/town/build eligibility
  - item price
  - CostPropItem
  - RewardPropItem
  - UnitActionPropUse.IsCreate
  - UnitActionRoleUpGrade
  - major breakthrough requirements
  - Goal141 materials/Fate/Luck
  - Realm/Grade writes

Custom behavior only answers:
  "Among the valid options already available, choose the progression-relevant one first."

===============================================================================

===============================================================================
FINAL CONCLUSION
===============================================================================
The final static mapping supports a conservative Smart Progression AI that never
fabricates a breakthrough and never creates an extra action.

It makes Player-Sect NPCs more intelligent by:
  - cultivating only while EXP can still advance,
  - recognizing the exact phase item configured for the next transition,
  - trying that exact owned item first when it is already a valid Action1029 candidate,
  - buying that exact item first when it is already a valid Action1004 market candidate,
  - then allowing the game's native UnitActionPropUse / UnitActionRoleUpGrade to decide,
  - and leaving major breakthrough to the already-buffed Goal101/Goal141 pipeline.

Mapping is now complete enough for a non-applied prototype.
No new V60 patch was applied in this report.

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 V60 — SMART PHASE AI PROTOTYPE AUDIT
===================================================================
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: NON-APPLIED PROTOTYPE — STATIC AUDIT PASS

BASELINE
--------
Source libil2cpp.so SHA256:
  c8c8e7ba9f4480d520c933e81c703f4286bb6a81e8528c1a1b2886168dcece06

The baseline lib remained byte-identical during this prototype build.
No hook bytes, program headers, or custom RX bytes were written into it.

PROTOTYPE LAYOUT
----------------
Link VMA: 0x08C78860, exactly at current custom RX end.
Compiled .text size: 0x340 / 832 bytes.
End VMA: 0x08C78BA0.
If later applied, custom RX p_filesz/p_memsz would grow:
  0x860 -> 0xBA0
and EOF would become:
  0x08A40860 -> 0x08A40BA0

Functions:
  get_progression_state    0x08C78860 size 0xC4
  action1031_smart_weight  0x08C78924 size 0x64
  action1029_smart_sort    0x08C78988 size 0x108
  action1004_smart_random  0x08C78A90 size 0x110

SHARED STATE HELPER
-------------------
get_progression_state(requester) uses only native V60 progression data/functions:
  requester +0x68 -> +0x30
  grade DynInt  +0x50  -> DynInt.get_value 0x057229C0
  EXP DynInt    +0x110 -> DynInt.get_value 0x057229C0
  global conf root      0x026A8D90
  ConfRoleGrade getter  0x026C52D8
  GetNextGradeItem      0x0405B1C8

Return states:
  0 unknown/end -> vanilla
  1 NEED_EXP
  2 MINOR_CAPPED
  3 MAJOR_CAPPED

Capped classification is based on:
  currentExp >= nextGradeItem.exp (+0x48)
Then:
  nextGradeItem.phase (+0x28) == 1 -> MAJOR_CAPPED
  otherwise                         -> MINOR_CAPPED

ACTION1031 WRAPPER
------------------
The trampoline replays displaced native:
  MADD W8,W0,W8,W21

60000 is used only when all are true:
  currentMonth > lastTrainsMonst
  requester belongs to current Player Sect
  get_progression_state == NEED_EXP

MINOR_CAPPED and MAJOR_CAPPED both fall back to native cultivation weight.
This prevents 60000 cultivation from monopolizing AI while EXP is already capped.

ACTION1029 WRAPPER
------------------
The wrapper is a normal BL replacement.
It first calls original:
  SortRandom<int> 0x06C86C50
with original incoming X0/X1/X2 untouched.

Then only for current Player-Sect + MINOR_CAPPED:
  obtains ConfRoleGrade singleton exactly like native callers
  calls GetPhaseProps 0x0405BD80
  scans returned List<int> exact propsIDs
  scans the already-created vanilla candidate List<int>
  swaps an existing matching propsID into index 0

It DOES NOT add a candidate.
It DOES NOT construct UnitActionPropUse.
It DOES NOT bypass CreateAction.
If native use rejects the first item, Action1029's original retry loop remains intact.

ACTION1004 WRAPPER
------------------
The wrapper first calls original:
  CommonTool.Random 0x057193C8
and stores the vanilla returned index.

Only for current Player-Sect + MINOR_CAPPED:
  obtains GetPhaseProps exact IDs
  scans final vanilla allBuyProps only
  each allBuyProps entry is read from List<T> items array
  entry +0x10 is compared as propsID
  if an exact ID already exists, wrapper returns that existing index
  otherwise returns vanilla random index

It DOES NOT add market stock.
It DOES NOT bypass market/town/build filtering.
It DOES NOT bypass price or CostPropItem.
It DOES NOT call RewardPropItem itself.
It DOES NOT force UnitActionPropUse.

ABI / STACK AUDIT
-----------------
AArch64 SP remains 16-byte aligned at every call boundary.
Frames:
  get_progression_state   0x50
  Action1031 wrapper      0x40
  Action1029 wrapper      0x60
  Action1004 wrapper      0x70

All callee-saved registers modified by each helper are saved/restored.
X18/platform register is untouched.
No unresolved relocations remain in linked prototype ELF.
All absolute BL/B targets disassemble to expected native/helper addresses.

---------------------------

  0x0559FCC4 file 0x0559BCC4

All three current source bytes were re-read from the baseline V60 and match expected values.

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 — V60 SMART PHASE + AGGRESSIVE CATCH-UP TEST
============================================================================
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: TEST BUILD — static verification PASS, Android runtime test required

BASE
----
Built directly on top of:
  V60 SMART_PHASE_AI_TEST
Base smart lib SHA256:
  be6a1f7e61e7cf6b85da3158b8b73712e83b93ac8d9111463502ad9bd9ec7fc9

New aggressive lib SHA256:
  cd1a79722423a4be22cb00a54883ced973acef4d4c93366f4665aac9b0d54e12

The previous Smart Phase AI remains active and byte-identical:
  - smart Action1031 cultivation stop at EXP cap
  - Action1029 exact owned phase-item priority
  - Action1004 exact market phase-item priority
  - existing V58 Player-Sect cultivation/Goal141/material catch-up features

GOAL
----
Increase progression THROUGHPUT across several different NPCs in the current Player Sect,
instead of allowing only one/few NPCs to consume most visible progression.

This build uses an aggressive rotating cohort scheduler. It does NOT write Realm/Grade,
does NOT grant phase items directly, and does NOT bypass native breakthrough requirements.

AGGRESSIVE COHORT SCHEDULER
---------------------------
Hook:
  WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit
  VA   0x0560A5E8
  file 0x056065E8
  old  88 0E D6 1A   ; SDIV W8,W20,W22
  new  6E B9 D9 14   ; B 0x08C78BA0

Scope guard:
  requester must pass existing is_player_school_npc @ 0x08C78080
  progression state from get_progression_state @ 0x08C78860 must be known/non-terminal

Selection uses the native realm-gap value already live in RunUnit and rotates by current month:
  realm gap >=3 : 100% selected
  realm gap ==2 : ~50% selected/month
  realm gap ==1 : ~33% selected/month
  realm gap ==0 : ~25% selected/month

The monthly cohort is derived from requester pointer hash XOR current month.
This deliberately avoids adding writable custom global state or changing the RX segment to RWX.
Therefore it is a rotating percentage cohort, NOT a hard global counter of exactly 5 or 8 NPCs.
With ~20 or more eligible Sect NPCs, the design should normally select around 5+ even when most
are same-gap, and more when many are seriously behind.

SELECTED NPC BUDGET
-------------------
For a selected catch-up NPC:
  helper makes downstream native pre-RNG W21 = 19
  native inclusive Random(19,20) remains unchanged
  final monthly Goal/opportunity budget = 19 or 20

For non-selected Player-Sect NPC:
  RunUnit budget stays native.

For NPCs outside current Player Sect:
  RunUnit is fully vanilla.

IMPORTANT:
  19-20 is Goal/opportunity budget, not guaranteed successful realm increases.
  Candidate selection, cooldowns, market availability, native CreateAction, phase item validation,
  Goal141 material routes, and breakthrough eligibility still apply.

WHY THIS IS AGGRESSIVE
----------------------
The V60 Smart Phase layer already ensures progression actions win when appropriate:
  NEED_EXP      -> Action1031 can receive weight 60000
  MINOR_CAPPED  -> exact phase item is first in Action1029 / Action1004 if vanilla-valid
  MAJOR_CAPPED  -> existing Goal101/Goal141 x20 + seekGradeNeedNum=1 + dungeon 100% remain

The new cohort scheduler gives several lagging Player-Sect NPCs many more chances in the same month,
while leaving other NPCs on normal scheduling. Severe lag (>=3) is always prioritized.

CUSTOM RX
---------
Before aggressive patch:
  file 0x08A40000 / VA 0x08C78000
  filesz=memsz=0xBA0
After:
  filesz=memsz=0xC60
  flags remain R-X
  align remains 0x4000

New helper:
  0x08C78BA0 aggressive_rununit_budget
  size 0xC0
  end  0x08C78C60

BINARY DIFF VS SMART_PHASE_AI_TEST
----------------------------------
Within the previous lib prefix, only 8 physical bytes differ:
  ELF p_filesz low bytes : 0xBA0 -> 0xC60
  ELF p_memsz low bytes  : 0xBA0 -> 0xC60
  RunUnit hook           : 4 bytes
Then exactly 0xC0 bytes are appended as helper code.

Previous Smart Phase hook bytes were rechecked unchanged:
  Action1031 0x055AFA8C -> B  0x08C78924
  Action1029 0x055AEF98 -> BL 0x08C78988
  Action1004 0x0559FCC4 -> BL 0x08C78A90

STATIC SAFETY
-------------
PASS:
  - no relocations in appended helper
  - AArch64 stack remains 16-byte aligned at call boundaries
  - callee-saved registers modified by helper are restored
  - X18 untouched
  - custom segment remains R-X, not RWX
  - exact RunUnit return target 0x0560A5EC
  - no direct Realm/Grade write
  - no free RewardPropItem path added
  - no forced breakthrough-success flag added
  - Player-Sect scope guard preserved
  - non-selected and outside-Sect paths fail safe to vanilla scheduler math

RUNTIME TEST
------------
Recommended first observation window: 1-3 in-game months.
Record 10-20 current-Sect NPCs before advancing month:
  name, realm/phase, and if possible current EXP.

Expected behavior:
  - more than one NPC should visibly progress per 1-3 month window
  - severely lagging NPCs should be especially active
  - Early/Middle EXP-capped NPCs should prefer their exact phase item
  - Late capped NPCs should get more opportunities for major breakthrough route

Watch for:
  - monthly processing slowdown if the Sect has a very large number of severely lagging NPCs
  - unusual action spam / social log volume
  - FC exactly on Next Month (would indicate scheduler helper ABI/runtime issue)

If CPU/month processing becomes too heavy, the first rollback/tuning target is ONLY the
RunUnit cohort hook; Smart Phase AI itself can remain.

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 V60 — PLAYER-SECT RUNTIME STAT DEEP MAPPING
================================================================================
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: MAPPING ONLY — NO PATCH / NO BYTE CHANGE
Input: libil2cpp.zip (V60 SMART_PHASE_AI_TEST)
libil2cpp.so SHA256: be6a1f7e61e7cf6b85da3158b8b73712e83b93ac8d9111463502ad9bd9ec7fc9
lib size: 0x08A40BA0 / 144,968,608 bytes
Custom RX: VA 0x08C78000 .. 0x08C78BA0, R-X

GOAL
----
Map a safe, non-stacking runtime stat boost for all existing and future NPCs of the
player's CURRENT Sect, without arbitrary Luck IDs, direct Realm writes, or monthly
multiplication of already-modified values.

================================================================================
1. DYNINT CORE — EXACT V60 RVAs
================================================================================
DynInt.get_value      0x057229C0
DynInt.get_allAddCall 0x057235FC
DynInt.get_allMulCall 0x057236CC
DynInt.AddValue       0x05723B58
DynInt.DelValue       0x05723CA0
DynInt.AddMul         0x05723E10
DynInt.DelMul         0x05723F58
DynObjectAddHandler::.ctor 0x05724B3C
DynObjectMulHandler::.ctor 0x05724C30

DynInt fields used by native runtime:
  +0x78 _allAddCall
  +0x80 _allMulCall
  +0x88 _allMulAddCall
  +0x90 _allEqualCall

Important multiplier semantics from DynInt.Value:
  cumulativeMul starts at 1.0
  for each applicable AddMul callback:
      cumulativeMul += callback()
  value is then multiplied by cumulativeMul.

Therefore:
  callback 0.0 -> no extra effect
  callback 0.5 -> +50% / ~x1.5
  callback 1.0 -> +100% / ~x2
  callback 2.0 -> +200% / ~x3

Native multipliers combine additively in the multiplier pool; they are not necessarily
multiplicative against every other percentage buff.

================================================================================
2. ADDMUL ENTRY / TAG SEMANTICS
================================================================================
Managed signature:
  DynInt.AddMul(DynObjectMulHandler call, object tag)

Native AddMul creates a small entry/wrapper and appends it to _allMulCall.
Observed entry layout:
  +0x10 = DynObjectMulHandler call
  +0x18 = tag object

AddMul itself DOES NOT perform duplicate prevention before appending.
Therefore blindly calling AddMul each month would stack callbacks.

The tag is a managed object, not restricted to a custom Luck/config ID.
A WorldUnit object can therefore be used conceptually as a per-unit tag.

A robust future dedup check can require BOTH:
  entry.tag == requester WorldUnit
  AND delegate invoke/method pointer == our unique custom callback helper
before deciding the modifier is already installed.

================================================================================
3. WORLDUNITDYNDATA IMPORTANT V60 OFFSETS
================================================================================
hp             +0x070
hpMax          +0x078
mp             +0x088
mpMax          +0x090
sp             +0x0A0
spMax          +0x0A8
dp             +0x0B0
dpMax          +0x0B8
attack         +0x0C8
defense        +0x0D0
crit           +0x0D8
critValue      +0x0E0
guard          +0x0E8
guardValue     +0x0F0
moveSpeed      +0x0F8
phycicalFree   +0x100
magicFree      +0x108
exp            +0x110
basisFire      +0x118 ... aptitude fields continue upward

Existing current Player-Sect filter remains:
  is_player_school_npc @ 0x08C78080
It rejects the player and compares the NPC School object against the player's CURRENT School.

================================================================================
4. NATIVE SCHOOLEFFECTUNITATTR — VANILLA NON-STACKING REFERENCE
================================================================================
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.Init                   0x05139D9C
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.UpdateAttr             0x05139EE8
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.Destroy                0x0513A8EC
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.OnRun                  0x0513AC6C
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.OnUnitActionSchoolJoin 0x0513AC70
SchoolEffectUnitAttr.OnUnitActionSchoolExit 0x0513AD78
SchoolEffectUnitAttr::.ctor                 0x0513AE78
<UpdateAttr>b__8_0 (mul value callback)      0x0513B110
<UpdateAttr>b__8_2 (add value callback)      0x0513B154
mul cleanup <UpdateAttr>b__1                0x0513B184
add cleanup <UpdateAttr>b__3                0x0513B1B0

Object layout observed:
  base SchoolEffectBase:
    +0x10 school
    +0x18 sloganItem
    +0x20 key
    +0x28 values
  SchoolEffectUnitAttr:
    +0x30 allUnitAttrDestroy
    +0x38 key1
    +0x40 key2

UpdateAttr lifecycle:
  1) invokes all old cleanup delegates in allUnitAttrDestroy
  2) clears/rebuilds current school-unit target collection
  3) resolves the requested WorldUnitDynData DynInt property
  4) creates DynObjectMulHandler or DynObjectAddHandler
  5) calls DynInt.AddMul / AddValue
  6) creates cleanup closure calling DelMul / DelValue
  7) stores cleanup action in allUnitAttrDestroy

Therefore VANILLA itself proves the safe pattern:
  remove old callback -> add fresh callback -> own cleanup lifetime.

Multiplier callback b__8_0 parses the numeric effect value and multiplies it by
0.01f. Constant at VA 0x015F4CE4 is exactly float 0.009999999776...
Thus an effect value 20 represents +0.20 in the AddMul pool.

Additive callback b__8_2 returns the parsed integer directly.

================================================================================
5. FULL NATIVE SCHOOL-EFFECT FACTORY ROUTE
================================================================================
WorldFactory.CreateSchoolEffect @ 0x05665D5C
Managed signature:
  SchoolEffectBase CreateSchoolEffect(string type)

WorldFactory contains an explicit "unitAttr" branch which allocates
SchoolEffectUnitAttr and calls its ctor at 0x0513AE78.

MapBuildSchool.InitSlogan @ 0x051D3E58
creates normal school effects approximately as:
  effectObject = WorldFactory.CreateSchoolEffect(effectType)
  effectObject.Init(thisSchool, sloganItem, fullEffectString)
  SchoolEffectBaseAdd(effectObject)

MapBuildSchool.SchoolEffectBaseAdd @ 0x051D6B50

SchoolEffectBase.Init @ 0x05136DD8 splits the effect string using '_'.
The observed UnitAttr consumption is consistent with:
  unitAttr_<dyn-property>_<mode>_<integer-value>
where mode is the native add/mul selection and value is read from values[2].

This FULL native route would automatically gain:
  immediate UpdateAttr
  join refresh
  exit refresh
  Destroy cleanup

BUT it requires safely supplying/owning managed effect strings/config and creating an
extra SchoolEffectBase object in the player's effect list. It is therefore elegant but
more invasive for a raw ARM64-only patch.

Status: YELLOW — fully mapped concept, but not first implementation choice.

================================================================================
6. SELF-CHECKING DYNINT DELEGATE ROUTE — NEW BEST CANDIDATE
================================================================================
Core concept for one stat:

  ensure_modifier_once(unit, dynInt, callback):
      if unit is not current Player-Sect:
          return

      list = dynInt.get_allMulCall()
      scan entries
      if entry.tag == unit AND entry.call.invoke_ptr == callback:
          return              // already installed, no stack

      delegate = new DynObjectMulHandler(...)
      delegate target = unit
      delegate invoke = callback
      dynInt.AddMul(delegate, tag=unit)

  callback(unit):
      if is_player_school_npc(unit):
          return desired_bonus_fraction
      return 0.0

Why this solves lifecycle without a custom Luck marker:
  - modifier is installed only once per DynInt / unit / callback
  - while NPC is in current Player Sect -> callback gives bonus
  - if NPC leaves -> same callback automatically returns 0
  - if same NPC rejoins -> same stored callback becomes active again
  - dedup tag uses the stable NPC object, so leave/rejoin does not create another copy
  - no arbitrary ConfRoleCreateFeature/Luck ID is required
  - no repeated base-stat multiplication is used

Different desired multipliers can use different tiny callback functions:
  e.g. callback_x2 -> return 1.0
       callback_x3 -> return 2.0
       callback_x1_5 -> return 0.5

Potential initial safe stat set for future design (NOT PATCHED):
  hpMax, attack, defense, phycicalFree, magicFree, optionally crit.
Avoid moveSpeed initially.

================================================================================
7. DELEGATE OBJECT CONSTRUCTION — IMPORTANT DETAILS
================================================================================
DynObjectMulHandler::.ctor @ 0x05724C30
stores normal IL2CPP delegate state.
Observed relevant delegate fields during invocation:
  +0x18 invoke_impl used by DynInt.Value
  +0x28 MethodInfo-like method metadata pointer
  +0x40 effective target used as callback x0

Vanilla SchoolEffectUnitAttr allocates DynObjectMulHandler via a known runtime class
pointer and initializes it with its b__8_0 method metadata.

A raw ARM64 custom helper cannot naturally have a new managed MethodInfo record.
A possible prototype route is therefore:
  - allocate a valid DynObjectMulHandler object using vanilla class metadata
  - initialize through the native delegate ctor with an existing compatible MethodInfo
  - replace the native invoke/method pointer fields with our custom RX callback
  - custom callback ignores the borrowed MethodInfo argument and uses target WorldUnit

This is technically plausible and matches the actual invocation ABI, but MUST be
prototype-compiled/disassembled and audited before any binary patch.

Status: YELLOW/GREEN concept; implementation proof still required.

================================================================================
8. GUARANTEED RETROFIT HOOK CANDIDATE
================================================================================
WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit @ 0x0560A15C
At entry:
  X19 = WorldUnitAIBase this (after prologue)
  [X19 + 0x10] = requester WorldUnit (previous mapping confirmed)

A narrow ensure-modifier call during monthly RunUnit can retrofit OLD saved Player-Sect
members automatically. The existing is_player_school_npc filter keeps non-player Sects vanilla.

Advantages:
  - old save needs no new-save regeneration
  - no need to enumerate all world units from a custom loader
  - future official members get installed naturally on a later monthly pass
  - Innate candidates that have not completed successful SchoolJoin should not pass the
    same current-School membership state used by the existing filter; successful join
    commits the School reference in the native join path.

Tradeoff:
  first visible runtime modifier may occur on the next NPC monthly processing cycle,
  not necessarily immediately on loading the save.

A later optional join-path call could make future official joins immediate, but is not
required for the first retrofit prototype.

================================================================================
9. INTERACTION WITH OLD +50% FIRST-JOIN PATCH
================================================================================
Existing old route:
  first successful selected auto Outer join -> +50% bundle over 37 DynInt stat containers.
It is permanent-style baseline modification and does not retrofit pre-existing members.

If a new uniform runtime multiplier is added to ALL current Player-Sect members while the
old +50% first-join remains active, newly joined NPCs can effectively receive BOTH layers:
  old +50% baseline + runtime modifier.
Old pre-patch members receive only the runtime layer.

Therefore an eventual implementation must deliberately choose policy:
  A) retain old +50% and accept stronger post-patch recruits, or
  B) stop giving +50% to future recruits and let the new runtime modifier become the
     uniform forward-looking system.

Already-saved NPCs that previously received +50% cannot be safely distinguished from
others using the current save data; no persistent "bonus applied" flag was stored.
Do NOT try to reverse +50% blindly.

================================================================================
10. BREAKTHROUGH GROWTH — PREVIOUS MYSTERY NOW CLOSED
================================================================================
ConfRoleGradeItem exact fields:
  +0x84 atkUp
  +0x88 defUp
  +0x8C hpUp
  +0x90 mpUp
  +0x94 spUp
  +0x98 crit
  +0x9C guard
  +0xA0 critV
  +0xA4 guardV
  +0xA8 pfrUp
  +0xAC mfrUp
  +0xB0 immortalPoint

Existing Player-Sect breakthrough increment wrapper applies:
  atkUp x2.5
  defUp x4
  hpUp x2
  mpUp x2
  spUp x2
  crit x2
  guard x4
  critV x5
  guardV x4
  pfrUp x4
  mfrUp x4
  immortalPoint x2

Owner/caller is UnitActionRoleUpGrade.GetAddAttr @ 0x05527340.
These multipliers affect BREAKTHROUGH INCREMENTS, not the NPC's entire already-existing total stat.
This explains why old members can still look close to vanilla despite large-looking growth multipliers.

================================================================================
11. CURRENT RECOMMENDATION — STILL MAPPING, NOT PATCHING
================================================================================
Best current architecture for a first prototype:

  Monthly current Player-Sect requester
      -> ensure runtime AddMul callback exists on selected DynInt stats
      -> tag with the WorldUnit object
      -> dedup by tag + unique callback pointer

  Runtime stat read
      -> callback checks is_player_school_npc(unit)
      -> current member: return bonus fraction
      -> outside current player Sect: return 0

  Future breakthrough
      -> keep existing legitimate native breakthrough flow + existing increment wrapper

Do NOT use:
  - arbitrary new Luck IDs without config/factory backing
  - currentStat *= N each month
  - global DynInt getter patches
  - global SchoolEffectUnitAttr modifier changes affecting every Sect
  - MoveSpeed multiplier in first test

================================================================================
12. REMAINING BEFORE "READY TO PATCH"
================================================================================
1) Build a NON-APPLIED AArch64 prototype of ensure_mul_once + self-check callbacks.
2) Validate managed delegate allocation/borrowed-MethodInfo field setup statically.
3) Validate dedup scanner against actual _allMulCall entry layout.
4) Choose exact RunUnit hook/trampoline instruction with preserved registers.
5) Decide old +50% first-join policy for future recruits.
6) Keep first runtime test small (Attack/Defense/HPMax only) before adding resist/crit.
7) Test save reload, leave Sect/rejoin, relocation, and 6-12 monthly skips.

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 — V60 PLAYER-SECT RUNTIME STAT PROTOTYPE MAPPING V2
==================================================================================
Date: 2026-08-31
Status: MAPPING / NON-APPLIED PROTOTYPE ONLY
IMPORTANT: NO BYTES WERE APPLIED TO libil2cpp.so.

GOAL
----
Map a safe route to retrofit runtime stat bonuses to EXISTING NPCs in the player's
current Sect, while also covering future official Sect members, WITHOUT:
- multiplying current stat values every month,
- stacking modifiers indefinitely,
- inventing a custom Luck/Fate ID,
- direct-writing Realm/Grade,
- modifying global NPC/Sect behavior.

SOURCE / BASE
-------------
Input libil2cpp.so:
  /mnt/data/libv60_inspect/libil2cpp.so
  SHA256: be6a1f7e61e7cf6b85da3158b8b73712e83b93ac8d9111463502ad9bd9ec7fc9
  Size:   0x08A40BA0 / 144,968,608 bytes
  Arch:   AArch64 Android IL2CPP

Current custom RX PT_LOAD:
  file start: 0x08A40000
  VA start:   0x08C78000
  size:       0xBA0
  end VA:     0x08C78BA0

Existing Player-current-Sect scope helper:
  is_player_school_npc @ 0x08C78080

==================================================================================
1. IMPORTANT CORRECTION FROM THE PREVIOUS MAPPING
==================================================================================

Previous working hypothesis treated DynInt AddMul/AddValue "tag" as an arbitrary
managed object and proposed using the WorldUnit pointer as the tag.

That is WRONG and is corrected here.

Metadata + evaluator mapping shows:
  DynInt.AddMul(DynObjectMulHandler call, System.String tag)
  DynInt.Value(..., System.String ignoreTag)

The tag is a managed STRING, not an arbitrary object.

DynInt evaluator behavior:
  for each mul entry:
      if (ignoreTag != entry.tag)
          invoke callback

Public DynInt.get_value normally reaches the internal evaluator with ignoreTag = null.
Therefore a custom AddMul entry must NOT blindly use a null tag, because null == null
causes that callback to be skipped in the normal get_value path.

Conclusion:
  - Do NOT use WorldUnit* as tag.
  - Do NOT use null as the custom AddMul tag.
  - Reuse a valid non-null managed string tag from the exact vanilla SchoolEffectUnitAttr
    AddMul path.

==================================================================================
2. VANILLA SCHOOL-EFFECT TAG ROUTE — EXACT STATIC HOLDER
==================================================================================

SchoolEffectUnitAttr.UpdateAttr uses DynInt.AddMul at:
  0x0513A68C -> DynInt.AddMul 0x05723E10

Immediately before the call:
  0x0513A678  ADRP ...
  0x0513A684  LDR holder from static slot
  0x0513A688  LDR X2,[holder]
  0x0513A68C  BL DynInt.AddMul

Exact static slot used for the managed string tag:
  0x07F7CB60

This same holder is used by multiple native DynInt AddMul paths.
For our purpose the strongest semantic choice is to borrow the SAME tag used by
SchoolEffectUnitAttr, because the desired modifier is itself a Sect runtime stat effect.

If some native stat calculation deliberately asks DynInt to ignore this School-effect
category/tag, our custom Player-Sect modifier should be ignored there too. Reusing the
native SchoolEffect tag preserves that behavior instead of inventing a new category.

Runtime fail-safe proposed for prototype:
  holder == null -> no install this cycle
  managed string == null -> no install this cycle
  retry naturally on a later RunUnit.

==================================================================================
3. DEDUP WITHOUT LUCK MARKERS AND WITHOUT TAG ABUSE
==================================================================================

DynInt.AddMul list layout confirmed:
  DynInt +0x80 = _allMulCall
  List   +0x10 = items array
  List   +0x18 = size
  array payload starts at +0x20

Each entry:
  +0x10 = DynObjectMulHandler delegate
  +0x18 = System.String tag

A created DynObjectMulHandler contains the original managed target and invoke code.
For our prototype, the target is the WorldUnit NPC and invoke_impl is our custom
self-check callback.

Correct V2 dedup identity:
  entry.delegate target == requester WorldUnit
  AND
  entry.delegate invoke_impl == our custom callback address

Therefore:
  first monthly pass -> install once
  later monthly pass -> scan finds exact delegate -> do nothing

No persistent custom Luck/Fate marker is required.
No custom writable global table is required.
No monthly multiplication of current stat is performed.

NOTE:
The constructor always stores the supplied target in the delegate object. The generated
instance-delegate path also uses the invocation target for callback dispatch. The V2
prototype currently scans the invocation-target field (+0x40). A future hardening pass
can additionally compare the constructor's original target field (+0x20) if desired.

==================================================================================
4. SELF-CHECKING CALLBACK — LEAVE/REJOIN BEHAVIOR
==================================================================================

DynInt multiplier evaluation is effectively based on:
  final multiplier contribution = 1.0 + sum(active AddMul callbacks)

Therefore prototype callback semantics can be:
  if is_player_school_npc(unit) == false:
      return 0.0

  x2 stat target:
      return 1.0

  x3 stat target:
      return 2.0

This is important:

NPC leaves Player Sect:
  callback remains registered but returns 0.0
  => no Player-Sect stat bonus

NPC rejoins Player Sect:
  same callback becomes active again automatically
  => bonus returns

Monthly processing:
  dedup sees the same unit+callback and does NOT add another entry.

This gives correctness without needing explicit removal on SchoolExit.
It does leave one small callback entry attached to each modified DynInt for that unit's
lifetime, but it is bounded (one per chosen stat/unit), not monthly unbounded growth.

==================================================================================
5. CLEANER EXISTING-SAVE RETROFIT HOOK FOUND
==================================================================================

WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit:
  start @ 0x0560A15C

Better hook candidate than the later realm-budget region:
  VA:   0x0560A2D4
  file: 0x056062D4

Original instruction:
  MOV X0,XZR
  bytes: E0 03 1F AA

Why this point is attractive:
  - IL2CPP/static initialization for this RunUnit path has already run.
  - caller X19 is still WorldUnitAIBase this.
  - requester is available at [X19 + 0x10].
  - it occurs before later early exits/goal-budget branches.
  - displaced behavior is only X0 = 0.
  - helper can restore X0 = 0 exactly before continuation.

Continuation:
  0x0560A2D8

Non-applied prototype branch to current custom-RX end 0x08C78BA0:
  instruction: 0x14D9BA33  bytes:       33 BA D9 14
  delta:       0x0366E8CC
  range:       valid AArch64 B range

NO hook byte has been written to the real lib.

==================================================================================
7. V2 DELEGATE CONSTRUCTION
==================================================================================

DynObjectMulHandler class holder used by vanilla:
  static slot 0x08011128

IL2CPP object allocation:
  0x02615BC8

DynObjectMulHandler::.ctor:
  0x05724C30

Borrowed vanilla MethodInfo holder currently used by prototype:
  0x08159E50

This is the MethodInfo used by:
  SchoolEffectUnitAttr.<UpdateAttr>b__8_0
  callback RVA 0x0513B110

V1 prototype overwrote both:
  delegate +0x10 method_ptr
  delegate +0x18 invoke_impl

V2 correction:
  KEEP constructor's method_ptr / MethodInfo intact
  overwrite ONLY:
      delegate +0x18 = custom ABI-compatible invoke_impl

Why this is cleaner:
  - managed MethodInfo remains internally consistent with method_ptr
  - managed target is rooted by normal delegate construction
  - only the native dispatch code pointer used by the DynInt evaluator is redirected

DynInt evaluator dispatch mapping confirms it directly loads:
  delegate +0x18 -> invoke_impl
  delegate +0x40 -> invocation target
  delegate +0x28 -> MethodInfo
  then BLR invoke_impl

Custom callback ignores the borrowed MethodInfo argument and uses the target as WorldUnit.

==================================================================================
8. GC / LIFETIME STATIC AUDIT
==================================================================================

Delegate constructor stores target through the normal generated managed-object path.
DynInt.AddMul allocates its wrapper and stores delegate/tag through IL2CPP GC write-barrier
paths.

Therefore once AddMul succeeds:
  DynInt list -> entry -> delegate -> target WorldUnit
provides a managed reference chain.

No custom RX writable global pointer is needed merely to keep the delegate alive.

Static V2 audit:
  - AArch64 link PASS
  - .text size 0x1D4
  - no relocations in linked prototype ELF
  - stack frames remain 16-byte aligned
  - callee-saved registers used by helper are restored
  - X18 untouched
  - hook branch range valid
  - original lib untouched

Prototype hashes:
  V60_RUNTIME_STAT_PROTO_V2_NONAPPLIED.s
    SHA256 b2f7c5d4517cb0a05c8c54c89b1863b8167c67c1aa39fedd0c34aac10503dd5b

  V60_RUNTIME_STAT_PROTO_V2_NONAPPLIED.elf
    SHA256 4c348b52c1cfc80b1098a223cde411f3dce9eeb4097d787e766ad11762550f9a

  V60_RUNTIME_STAT_PROTO_V2_NONAPPLIED.dis
    SHA256 234179e3d1b4ac5b16a2ab8ccfd576b60f9af48dbab72657294d9d240bdebab9

==================================================================================
9. COVERAGE: EXISTING MEMBERS, NEW DISCIPLES, AND THE ~140 INNATE RESERVE
==================================================================================

The hook first checks existing:
  is_player_school_npc @ 0x08C78080

Therefore:

A) Existing official Player-Sect NPC
   -> next normal RunUnit processing
   -> passes Player-Sect filter
   -> modifier can be installed once

B) New Innate/reserve candidate not yet an official Player-Sect NPC
   -> filter does not pass
   -> no runtime stat modifier installed

C) Candidate later becomes an official Disciple/member
   -> enters normal Player-Sect WorldUnit/AI lifecycle
   -> next RunUnit can install modifier once

D) NPC leaves the Player Sect
   -> callback self-check returns 0
   -> bonus deactivates without destructive base-stat writes

This is the intended coverage model for the user's observed Sect-master recruitment cycle.

==================================================================================
10. RELATION TO OLD +50% FIRST-JOIN AND BREAKTHROUGH GROWTH PATCHES
==================================================================================

Nothing in this mapping changes the existing patches.

Old first-join route:
  selected successful first-time auto Outer join
  -> +50% over 37 DynInt stat containers once

Old breakthrough route:
  UnitActionRoleUpGrade.GetAddAttr @ 0x05527340
  -> scales breakthrough INCREMENTS for current Player-Sect NPCs

The runtime retrofit is a third layer:
  existing total/runtime stat modifier

IMPORTANT future design question:
If old +50% first-join remains active AND the new runtime modifier is added, certain future
recruits may receive both the old permanent +50% first-join adjustment and the new runtime
modifier, while pre-existing members receive only the runtime modifier.

This is a POLICY issue, not a mapping uncertainty.
Recommendation before eventual implementation:
  consider disabling the future +50% first-join bonus when the runtime layer becomes the
  canonical all-member stat bonus.

Do NOT attempt to divide old NPC stats by 1.5 because there is no persistent historical
marker proving exactly which NPC received the old first-join bundle.

==================================================================================
11. PRIMARY REMAINING BLOCKER — METHODINFO/TARGET TYPE CONSISTENCY
==================================================================================

This is the main reason the prototype is NOT yet READY TO PATCH.

Current V2 trick:
  - allocate a genuine DynObjectMulHandler
  - borrow MethodInfo for SchoolEffectUnitAttr.<UpdateAttr>b__8_0
  - pass WorldUnit as the managed delegate target
  - override invoke_impl to our custom callback

Static ctor disassembly does not show a hard declaring-type validation of the supplied target,
and DynInt later dispatches using invoke_impl + target directly.
So the technique is mechanically plausible.

However, the borrowed MethodInfo DECLARING TYPE belongs to SchoolEffectUnitAttr while our
managed target is WorldUnit.

Even if the current generated ctor accepts it, this is an internal managed-type inconsistency
that should not be promoted to GREEN without either:

Option A — preferred:
  Find a native DynObjectMulHandler MethodInfo whose declaring/target type can legitimately be
  a WorldUnit (or compatible object), then borrow that MethodInfo instead.

Option B:
  Build/use a correctly typed managed closure object that stores the WorldUnit reference and
  borrow a MethodInfo belonging to that closure type.

Option C — higher risk:
  Keep the current target/MethodInfo mismatch and rely on invoke_impl override.
  This should be runtime-tested only after A/B are exhausted.

Current status of this item:
  YELLOW — plausible, static-clean, but not approved for final patch.

==================================================================================
12. PERFORMANCE / MONTHLY COST
==================================================================================

For a modified official Sect NPC, every RunUnit pass would:
  - Player-Sect filter
  - for each selected stat, scan the existing small _allMulCall list
  - normally find the already-installed delegate and return

This is bounded work, not accumulating monthly callbacks.

For ~140 reserve Innates:
  those that are not official Player-Sect members fail scope and do not enter installation.

If eventually applying many more than 3 stats, the install/check cost should be measured on
device before expanding to all 37+ properties. A first runtime test should use only core combat
stats to establish monthly processing cost.

UnitDataBase.InitData is the critical bridge.

Source WorldUnit dynamic property container:
  source WorldUnitData +0x30 -> WorldUnitDynData

It repeatedly loads a WorldUnitDynData DynInt, calls:
  DynInt.get_value @ 0x057229C0

and then transfers that evaluated value into the battle UnitData-side property.

Exact examples:

A) MAX RESOURCE VALUES
  source hpMax +0x78
    0x050023AC LDR X8,[WorldUnitData,#0x30]
    0x050023AC..0x050023BC source +0x78 -> DynInt.get_value
    destination battle property from UnitDataBase +0x70

  source mpMax +0x90 -> battle destination +0x78
  source spMax +0xA8 -> battle destination +0x80
  source dpMax +0xB8 -> battle destination +0x88

B) CURRENT RESOURCE VALUES
  source hp +0x70 -> evaluated and stored into UnitDataBase +0x60
  source mp +0x88 -> UnitDataBase +0x64
  source sp +0xA0 -> UnitDataBase +0x68
  source dp +0xB0 -> UnitDataBase +0x6C

C) ATTACK
  0x05002508  LDR X0,[sourceDyn,#0xC8]   ; WorldUnitDynData.attack DynInt
  0x05002518  BL  DynInt.get_value
  destination battle DynInt from UnitDataBase +0xA8

D) DEFENSE
  0x05002538  LDR X0,[sourceDyn,#0xD0]   ; WorldUnitDynData.defense DynInt
  0x05002548  BL  DynInt.get_value
  destination battle DynInt from UnitDataBase +0xB0

E) OTHER COMBAT VALUES CONFIRMED IN THE SAME ROUTE
  moveSpeed     source +0xF8
  crit          source +0xD8
  critValue     source +0xE0
  guard         source +0xE8
  guardValue    source +0xF0
  physicalFree  source +0x100
  magicFree     source +0x108

For example:
  0x05002658 source +0x100 -> DynInt.get_value  ; physical resistance/free
  0x05002688 source +0x108 -> DynInt.get_value  ; magic resistance/free

This proves the intended runtime modifier is not merely cosmetic UI data:

  WorldUnitDynData DynInt
      -> AddMul/AddValue callbacks evaluated
      -> DynInt.get_value
      -> battle UnitData receives evaluated result

Therefore Attack/Defense/HP Max/RES runtime modifiers should affect actual NPC battle stats
when that NPC battle unit is initialized from the WorldUnit.

Confidence:
  VERY HIGH / direct binary bridge confirmed.

===============================================================================
4. RUNUNIT LIFECYCLE — NO CUSTOM GLOBAL 140-NPC LOOP IS REQUIRED
===============================================================================

WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit:
  0x0560A15C

Direct BL xref scan of the main executable found exactly one direct caller:
  0x0523E93C -> 0x0560A15C

Caller owner:
  <>c__DisplayClass23_0.<RunUnit>b__1 @ 0x0523E708

This closure belongs to:
  WorldUnitAIMgr.RunUnit @ 0x0523CFB0

The manager creates/uses the closure as part of its normal unit-AI processing. Thus the
proposed retrofit hook at WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit does not need a custom loop across every
world unit or every Innate candidate.

Monthly integration remains native:
  WorldUnitAIMgr.Init registers at WorldRun order NPC:
    0x0523C734 MOV W1,#2
    0x0523C740 BL WorldRunMgr.On

The proposed V3 hook remains:
  WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit +0x178
  VA   0x0560A2D4
  file 0x056062D4
  original MOV X0,XZR
  bytes E0 03 1F AA
  continuation 0x0560A2D8

V3 helper first does:
  requester = [AIBase +0x10]
  is_player_school_npc(requester)

Only when that passes does it inspect/install selected DynInt modifiers.

Performance implication:
  - non-Player-Sect unit: one narrow scope check, then return
  - reserve/Innate not yet official member: same early return, assuming it does not pass the
    current-School identity used by is_player_school_npc
  - official Player-Sect member: check selected stat lists and install only if missing
  - after install: bounded per-stat dedup scan, no growing monthly callback stack

This is substantially safer than adding a new custom global enumeration of ~140 candidates.

===============================================================================
5. OLD +50% FIRST-JOIN ROUTE — CLEAN RETIREMENT PATH CONFIRMED
===============================================================================

Current live V60 bytes at first-join patch site:
  VA   0x055367E0
  file 0x055327E0
  bytes 1A 07 DD 14
  meaning: B 0x08C78448

Original vanilla instruction from the audited pre-hook mapping:
  LDR X0,[X20,#0x18]
  bytes 80 0E 40 F9

Current branch/xref audit:

  0x055367E0 --B--> 0x08C78448       first-join wrapper
  0x08C7848C --BL-> 0x08C7815C       +50% 37-stat bundle
  0x08C78190 --BL-> 0x08C78124       +50% one-stat helper

Critically:
  - 0x08C78448 has only this one inbound main-code branch.
  - 0x08C7815C has only the first-join wrapper as its inbound direct branch/call.
  - 0x08C78124 is only used by the 37-stat bundle in this custom surface.

Therefore, if the runtime all-member modifier becomes the canonical system later, the cleanest
way to stop FUTURE first-join +50% is simply:

  restore at 0x055367E0:
    80 0E 40 F9   ; LDR X0,[X20,#0x18]

The old custom helper can remain dormant for rollback/reference; it does not need to be erased.

Important save behavior:
  - this only stops the old +50% from being applied to future eligible joins.
  - it does NOT reverse any stat values already permanently modified in existing saves.
  - blindly dividing old NPC stats by 1.5 remains unsafe because no historical marker identifies
    exactly which NPC previously received the old bundle.

Confidence:
  VERY HIGH for future-hook retirement route.

===============================================================================
6. EFFECT ON THE USER'S ~140 INNATE/RESERVE CYCLE
===============================================================================

Expected V3 runtime architecture:

A) generated Innate/reserve candidate, not official current Player-Sect member
   -> may still have normal world AI processing
   -> runtime helper executes scope check only
   -> is_player_school_npc false
   -> NO stat modifier installation

B) candidate becomes official Disciple/member 1-2 months later
   -> current School identity now matches Player current Sect
   -> next normal WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit can install the runtime modifier once

C) old official member already present in the save
   -> next normal RunUnit can retrofit modifier once

D) official NPC leaves current Player Sect
   -> already-installed self-check callback returns 0 contribution
   -> runtime Player-Sect bonus becomes inactive without destructive stat writes

E) NPC later rejoins
   -> same callback can become active again
   -> dedup prevents adding a second copy

This matches the intended distinction between reserve population and actual Sect members.

===============================================================================
7. FORMULA / BALANCE CONSEQUENCE — ADDMUL IS STRONGER THAN BASE-ONLY SCALING
===============================================================================

DynInt evaluator mapping established earlier:
  effective value is built from base/dynamic base plus AddValue contributions,
  then AddMul contributes a factor approximately:

    factor = 1.0 + sum(active multiplier callbacks)

Thus:
  callback 0.5 -> approximately x1.5
  callback 1.0 -> approximately x2
  callback 2.0 -> approximately x3

Important:
  AddMul scales the value after earlier additive contributions; it is not merely
  `baseValue * multiplier` in isolation.

Therefore final combat tuning should remain moderate, especially for HP/Attack/Defense/RES.
The V3 sample values (Attack x2 / Defense x2 / HP Max x3) are only proof-of-route values and
should NOT automatically become final balance values.

===============================================================================

===============================================================================

GREEN / VERY HIGH CONFIDENCE:
  [x] current Player-Sect filter exists and is narrow
  [x] DynInt AddMul/AddValue routes mapped
  [x] non-null System.String tag semantics mapped
  [x] type-correct managed carrier/MethodInfo route found in V3
  [x] GC ownership chain has no custom writable global requirement
  [x] bounded dedup design; no monthly callback accumulation
  [x] clean RunUnit hook candidate and displaced instruction
  [x] NPC UI reads live DynInt
  [x] actual battle initialization reads live WorldUnitDynData DynInt values
  [x] RunUnit has one direct manager call path in normal AI lifecycle
  [x] monthly NPC-phase registration confirmed
  [x] old future +50% first-join route can be retired by restoring one vanilla instruction
  [x] no need to reverse historical +50% values

STILL YELLOW / REQUIRES DEVICE TEST OR USER BALANCE DECISION:

  [ ] final set of boosted stats
  [ ] final multiplier values

STATIC ARCHITECTURE STATUS:
  READY FOR A CONTROLLED TEST BUILD DESIGN,
  but NOT yet claimed production-safe until Android runtime testing is performed.

No binary patch was made during V4 mapping.

=================================================================================
2. IMPORTANT V3 HARDENING FOUND DURING THIS PASS
=================================================================================

The first reconstructed V3 prototype used X8 to hold a runtime-metadata holder pointer, called:
  IL2CPP_METADATA_INIT @ 0x0261597C
and then read [X8] afterward.

That is unsafe because X8 is caller-saved under AAPCS64. A callee may clobber it.

CORRECTION:
  - store each metadata-holder pointer in callee-saved X28 before BL metadata-init;
  - call metadata-init with X0=X28;
  - dereference [X28] afterward.

The corrected V3 prototype was rebuilt and disassembled.
No relocations remain.

GC hardening:
  carrier+0x10 is a MANAGED WorldUnit reference.
  Correct prototype sequence is:
      STR requester,[carrier,#0x10]
      ADD X0,carrier,#0x10
      MOV X1,requester
      BL 0x02615928

This matches the generated IL2CPP write-barrier assignment pattern instead of relying on a raw
pointer store alone.

=================================================================================
3. CORRECT V3 DEDUP IDENTITY
=================================================================================

V2 dedup compared delegate.target directly with WorldUnit.
That is no longer correct in V3 because delegate.target is the carrier.

V3 scan:
  DynInt +0x80 -> _allMulCall List
  List +0x10   -> items array
  List +0x18   -> size
  wrapper +0x10 -> DynObjectMulHandler delegate

For each delegate:
  delegate +0x18 = invoke_impl
  delegate +0x40 = target carrier
  carrier  +0x10 = requester WorldUnit

Exact V3 dedup condition:
  delegate.invoke_impl == custom_callback
  AND
  delegate.target != null
  AND
  delegate.target->unit == requester

If all match, do not install a second modifier.

Therefore monthly RunUnit passes do not grow callbacks indefinitely.

=================================================================================
4. SELF-CHECK CALLBACK / LEAVE-REJOIN
=================================================================================

Custom callback ABI:
  float callback(carrier* target, MethodInfo*)

Flow:
  carrier null -> 0
  carrier.unit null -> 0
  is_player_school_npc(carrier.unit) == false -> 0
  otherwise return the configured multiplier contribution

DynInt multiplier contribution semantics already mapped:
  callback 0.0 -> no Player-Sect bonus
  callback 0.5 -> roughly x1.5 total factor
  callback 1.0 -> roughly x2
  callback 2.0 -> roughly x3

The callback remains attached if NPC leaves Sect, but returns zero.
If the same NPC later rejoins the player's current Sect, it becomes active again without adding a
second entry because dedup follows carrier.unit + invoke_impl.

=================================================================================
5. HP CURRENT/MAX CAUTION — FIRST TEST SET REVISED
=================================================================================

Battle initialization reads WorldUnit HP MAX and CURRENT HP from different DynInt fields.

UnitDataBase.InitData battle bridge:
  source hpMax DynInt  WorldUnitDynData +0x78 -> get_value around 0x050023BC
  source current hp    WorldUnitDynData +0x70 -> get_value around 0x05002478

The max value is copied into the battle-side max container, while current HP is read separately.
Therefore boosting hpMax alone can create a unit whose current HP is still based on the old value.

Boosting both current hp and hpMax may preserve the ratio at battle initialization, BUT current-HP
write-back/persistence after battle has not yet been fully mapped. A multiplicative callback on a
mutable current-resource field could create unwanted feedback if battle results are later written
into its base value.

Conclusion for FIRST controlled test design:
  DO NOT include HP/hpMax yet.

Preferred first proof set:
  Attack  +0xC8
  Defense +0xD0

These are read as live DynInt values into battle and do not have a mutable current/max pair.

Physical/Magic RES remain plausible later additions:
  +0x100 phycicalFree
  +0x108 magicFree
but should be added only after the minimal Attack/Defense route proves stable.

Avoid MoveSpeed initially.

=================================================================================

=================================================================================

UnitDataBase.InitData reads live WorldUnitDynData values through:
  DynInt.get_value @ 0x057229C0

Confirmed source offsets/calls include:
  Attack  +0xC8, read near 0x05002518
  Defense +0xD0, read near 0x05002548
  Physical RES +0x100, read near 0x05002668
  Magic RES    +0x108, read near 0x05002698

Thus runtime AddMul on Attack/Defense changes the value that is snapshotted into the NPC's battle
data when battle data is initialized. It is not UI-only.

13. REMAINING MAPPING BEFORE DEVICE TEST
=================================================================================

Static architecture is now strongly mapped, but two areas remain worth auditing before producing a
real test lib:

1) CURRENT HP persistence/write-back after battle
   Determine whether battle current HP is written back into WorldUnit hp.baseValue/effective value.
   Until known, do not runtime-multiply current hp/hpMax.

2) Exact metadata-init runtime cost under repeated RunUnit
   The helper is idempotent/thread-safe in generated IL2CPP usage, but a future optimization may
   avoid repeated metadata-init calls once a stat already has our delegate. Since dedup scan occurs
   before install, existing modified NPCs do NOT reach metadata-init after the first successful
   install for that stat. Thus steady-state overhead is already bounded.

STATIC STATUS AFTER V5:
  Runtime Attack/Defense retrofit architecture: READY FOR CONTROLLED TEST-BUILD IMPLEMENTATION
  HP runtime multiplier: NOT READY; continue mapping persistence/write-back first
  Production-safe claim: NO, requires Android device runtime test
