Role mapping inside SchoolWarAttackWin:

  Around 0x05176084:
    X20 = schoolWarData.defendSchool

  Around 0x0517702C..0x05177054:
    obtains defendSchool.get_buildData()
    loads npcSchoolMain +0x78
    resolves WorldUnit via WorldUnitMgr.GetUnit
    resulting WorldUnit is kept in X26

Therefore:
  X20 = old defending School
  X26 = defending Sect Master WorldUnit

Later:
  0x051778E0..0x05177918
    calls defendSchool.GetAllSchools(false)
    obtains another related/fallback School in X28

Therefore:
  X28 = destination/replacement related School

Critical final transfer sequence:
  0x05177DA8  defender School get_buildData
  0x05177DD4  SchoolData.ExitSchool @ 0x040ACAC8 using defender School + X26 unitID
  0x05177DE4  destination X28 get_buildData
  0x05177DF8  load destination schoolID
  0x05177DFC  WRITE X26.UnitInfoData.schoolID = destination schoolID
  0x05177E04  IL2CPP GC/write-barrier-related generated call
  0x05177E08  B 0x05177F1C

This transfer bypasses UnitActionSchoolJoin.

================================================================================
5. WHY THE SCHOOL WAR BYPASS MATTERS TO RUNTIME STATS
================================================================================

The runtime Attack/Defense callbacks themselves remain functionally safe because every
evaluation calls is_player_school_npc, which resolves current School membership.

After SchoolWar writes a new schoolID:
  WorldUnitData.get_school can resolve the new School;
  callback immediately returns:
    active contribution if current Player School;
    0 otherwise.

So LIVE COMBAT STATS stay correct.

But UnitPower.TotalPower has a per-NPC cache with up to approximately 59 months TTL.

Therefore:
  a direct SchoolWar transfer can leave a stale strategic/AI power score even after
  the runtime callback has changed membership state.

Potential cases:
  - leader leaves current Player School -> combat bonus goes off, cached strong power remains;
  - leader enters current Player School -> combat bonus can become active, cached weak power remains.

This is the same class of issue already solved for normal Join/Exit, but this path bypasses
those hooks.

================================================================================
6. NARROW SCHOOL WAR CACHE INVALIDATION DESIGN
================================================================================

Clean hook candidate:
  VA   0x05177E08
  file 0x05173E08

Current instruction:
  B 0x05177F1C

Current / rollback bytes:
  45 00 00 14

Why this site is strong:
  - direct schoolID write has completed;
  - generated write barrier has completed;
  - old School is still available in X20;
  - transferred Sect Master WorldUnit is still X26;
  - new destination School is still X28;
  - original instruction is itself an unconditional branch;
  - helper can return directly to the same original continuation.

Recommended scope:
  if is_player_school(X20) OR is_player_school(X28):
      invalidate_unitpower_cache(X26)

Then:
  branch to original 0x05177F1C.

This does NOT:
  - change the war result;
  - change schoolID;
  - change membership;
  - clear global UnitPower cache;
  - change any NPC stat;
  - buff another Sect.

It only removes a stale cached power record for the transferred leader when the transfer
touches current Player School.

================================================================================

================================================================================
8. NORMAL IN-WORLD LIFECYCLE COVERAGE AFTER V12
================================================================================

Mapped runtime-stat / UnitPower synchronization points:

A) Existing current Player-Sect NPC
  RunUnit first modifier install
  -> targeted UnitPower cache remove

B) Normal successful SchoolJoin/rejoin
  -> post-commit targeted cache remove

C) Normal successful SchoolExit
  -> post-commit targeted cache remove

D) Relocation members migrated to destination main School
  -> relocation internally uses Exit -> Join for formal post groups
  -> covered by B/C

E) Relocation units remaining in child branch
  -> runtime callback deactivates by exact-School mismatch
  -> V11 post-relocation child-list targeted cache cleanup

F) School War direct Sect-Master transfer
  -> direct schoolID write bypasses normal Join
  -> V12 SchoolWar post-write targeted cache cleanup

This gives substantially broader ordinary-gameplay coverage without changing vanilla
School assignment logic.

================================================================================
9. LOAD / FIX / DEBUG PATHS
================================================================================

The other exact direct schoolID writes belong to:
  - version/update repair;
  - cache-data-back migration;
  - WorldMgr world-entry FixData;
  - GM/debug commands.

They are not good candidates for separate in-world runtime-stat hooks.

Why:
  - load/fix paths happen before or during world-entry normalization;
  - WorldUnitData.get_school resolves against the corrected persistent schoolID;
  - existing-member first runtime modifier install already invalidates that WorldUnit's
    UnitPower cache when the unit later runs through the Player-Sect retrofit path;
  - GM/debug paths are not ordinary gameplay lifecycle.

No extra patch is recommended for these paths at this stage.

================================================================================
10. REMAINING CAVEATS
================================================================================

1) Exact direct-write scan is strong but native IL2CPP can also mutate data through generic
   serializer/reflection/setter machinery. Those are primarily load/save/data-repair concerns,
   not ordinary interactive School transfer paths.

2) SchoolWarAttackWin transfer currently maps the defending Sect Master specifically.
   Other SchoolWar outcomes should still be audited if there are independent functions for
   surrender, annihilation, merge, branch deletion, or counterattack completion that mutate
   School identity by a structurally different path.

3) The ~140 Innate/reserve recruitment population is NOT resolved by GetPostUnit(0).
   Do not infer their exact pre-disciple membership state from this mapping.

4) All current runtime-stat plans remain NON-APPLIED and require Android device testing before
   any production use.

  per-department Initiate cap 40

This makes values around 140 structurally possible even though the visible helper constant is 40.

5. PUBLIC SOURCE CROSS-CHECK FOR manageOut STRUCTURE

  school.buildData.postData.postElders[department].manageOut

and identifies:

    Dictionary<SchoolDepartmentType, DataBuildSchool.PostData.PostElders>

The public helper adds/removes IDs from:
  postElders[department].manageOut

This exactly matches the Android native enumeration and +0x28 manageOut count path.

================================================================================
6. THE "7" HELPER IS outYearAddUnitCount
================================================================================

Current custom helper:
  0x08C784C8

Native call replaced/fallback:
  0x03AB2ED4

Disassembly of 0x03AB2ED4 shows:
  MOV W1,#14
  call ConfSchoolPostParam lookup
  return item.value

Public SchoolPostParam ID 14:
  key   = outYearAddUnitCount
  value = -1

Therefore the current Player-Sect helper changes:
  outYearAddUnitCount
from native/config result
to:
  7

This is much more precise than the older label "Stage A target 7".

================================================================================
7. WHY -1 CAN PRODUCE A LARGE ANNUAL BURST
================================================================================

MapBuildSchoolPostOut.AddOutUnit stores the result of outYearAddUnitCount in W21.

The loop checks W21 for exactly zero.
When the value is a positive finite count, the loop can terminate after the intended
finite number of recruitment attempts/success-path consumption.

With native -1:
  decrementing remains negative (-1, -2, -3, ...)
  and never naturally reaches zero.

Therefore native -1 behaves as an effectively unlimited annual recruitment count,
with termination instead coming from:
  - per-department manageOut numMax
  - candidate list exhaustion
  - validation/failure conditions
  - department iteration completion

This architecture naturally supports a very large annual fill.

================================================================================
8. CRITICAL SCOPE RISK IN BOTH CURRENT HELPERS
================================================================================

Both current custom helpers:
  annual outYearAddUnitCount = 7
  Initiate numMax = 40

scope themselves using:
  MapBuildSchoolPostBase.this +0x10 -> school
  is_player_school(school) @ 0x08C78048

is_player_school uses the player's RESOLVED current School.

Previously mapped:
  for a Sect Master, WorldUnitData.get_school() can return GetTopSchool(...).

Therefore a possible mismatch exists:

  PostOut.school = exact branch/local MapBuildSchool
  Player resolved School = top/headquarters MapBuildSchool

If these objects differ:
  is_player_school(PostOut.school) == false

Then BOTH helpers fall back to native:
  outYearAddUnitCount -> native/config (-1 in public config)
  numMax              -> native ConfSchoolPost.numMax

This is currently the strongest architectural explanation for:
  "Player Sect looked modded, but after about one year Initiate suddenly jumped by ~140"

Especially after branch/headquarters/relocation state exists.

STATUS:
  Strong hypothesis, not yet runtime-proven for the user's exact save object identities.
================================================================================
9. INITIATE -> DISCIPLE SOURCE IS EXACTLY postType 6
================================================================================

MapBuildSchoolPostIn.AddOutUnit:
  RVA 0x0513F8B4

At:
  0x0513FC38  MOV W1,#6
  0x0513FC40  BL SchoolData.GetPostUnit

So promotion candidates are the formal Initiate list only.

Target rank:
  ConfSchoolPost.GetItem(..., post=5)
  later UnitActionSchoolSetPostType uses postType 5

Confirmed lifecycle:
  Initiate (6) -> Disciple (5)

================================================================================
10. DISCIPLE CAP IS ALSO PER DEPARTMENT
================================================================================

MapBuildSchoolPostIn.AddOutUnit also enumerates:
  postData.postElders dictionary

For each current PostElders:
  +0x20 = manageIn
  manageIn.Count is used as current Disciple occupancy for that department.

The postType 5 ConfSchoolPostItem.numMax is loaded and stored as the department capacity.

Current native hook:
  0x0513FB00 -> 0x08C7864C

Exact custom helper disassembly:
  if exact Player School:
      W9 = 30
  else:
      W9 = ConfSchoolPostItem.numMax (+0x28)

IMPORTANT CORRECTION:
  The current uploaded lib's Disciple cap helper is 30, not 20.

Therefore current intended custom capacity is:
  Disciple/manageIn <= 30 PER DEPARTMENT

Potential aggregate across five real departments:
  up to ~150 formal Disciples
if all departments are active and the helper applies.

================================================================================
11. PROMOTION SELECTION / SCORING
================================================================================

Promotion source:
  SchoolData.GetPostUnit(6) -> Initiates

Candidates are converted to WorldUnit objects and scored.

MapBuildSchoolPostBase.GetUnitPower @ 0x05145438 receives:
  unit
  herosAddRate
  reserveHeroesAddRate
  randomPowerRateMin
  randomPowerRateMax

It starts from:
  UnitPower.TotalPower(unit)

Then applies:
  TaoistHeart Hero bonus
  Reserve Hero bonus
  random percentage adjustment

Public SchoolPostParam reference:
  inAddUnitParam3 = -20
  inAddUnitParam4 = +20
  inAddUnitParam5 = -1

Therefore promotion is power-ranked with Hero/Reserve-Hero preference and random jitter,
not a simple realm-only promotion.

Runtime/player-Sect Attack/Defense boosts, when eventually applied, can influence this ranking
indirectly through UnitPower.TotalPower. They do NOT directly write Sect post type.

================================================================================
12. TIMING
================================================================================

MapBuildSchoolPostOut.OnSystemEnd1 contains the annual/date gate that leads to the large
Initiate recruitment pass.

MapBuildSchoolPostIn.OnSystem uses the date/month state and inStartMonth for the
Initiate -> Disciple process.

Public reference:
  inStartMonth = 1

This matches the observed pattern:
  end-of-year / annual cycle -> Initiate population can jump
  following month(s)         -> some strong Initiates become Disciples

================================================================================
13. WHAT THE ~140 OBSERVATION NOW TELLS US
================================================================================

The exact current Player-main helper path intends:
  annual recruit count = 7
  Initiate capacity     = 40 per department

A jump of roughly 140 is therefore NOT evidence that "40 was ignored globally";
40 was never a global cap.

However, +140 in one annual cycle is still too large for a correctly applied finite
outYearAddUnitCount=7 in one AddOutUnit invocation.

So at least one of the following must be true in the observed save/build:

A) Exact-School helper scope failed
   -> native outYearAddUnitCount path used
   -> native -1 can bulk-fill departments.

B) Multiple branch/local PostOut instances ran
   -> only exact top/current object received helper behavior.

C) The observation came from a save/build before this exact SMART_PHASE binary/helper state.

D) Another formal-member fill path ran in the same annual/system transition.

Current strongest candidate:
  A/B — branch/top-school object mismatch.

================================================================================
14. NEXT MAPPING TARGET
================================================================================

Do NOT patch yet.

Next mapping should inspect:
  - how many MapBuildSchoolPostOut instances execute for one player Sect family per annual cycle;
  - exact object identity of PostOut.school for main vs child branches;
  - whether Council Hall Initiate display is local branch count or family aggregate;
  - whether player Sect Master get_school() returning TopSchool causes Stage-A helper misses;
  - all other paths that add IDs to SchoolData.npcOut.

This is the clean way to solve the remaining "~140 despite target 7" discrepancy.

================================================================================
1. PREVIOUS MAPPING STATE CARRIED FORWARD
================================================================================

From V9-V12, the current canonical Player-Sect identity model remains:

Persistent membership identity:
  DataUnit.UnitInfoData.schoolID +0x30

Runtime cached School object:
  WorldUnitData._school +0x38

Resolver:
  WorldUnitData.get_school @ 0x056478DC

  -> resolves NPC and player current School objects
  -> exact current-School pointer comparison
  -> intentionally NOT blanket GetTopSchool family-wide scope.

V12 direct schoolID-write audit remains relevant. Normal gameplay writers already mapped:
  UnitActionSchoolJoin.OnCreate @ 0x05536028 persistent write

V12 remaining yellow item relevant to this pass:

================================================================================
2. CURRENT AGGRESSIVE V2 HOOK — WHAT IT REALLY SETS
================================================================================

WorldUnitAIBase.RunUnit:
  @ 0x0560A15C

Aggressive V2 hook:
  0x0560A5E8 -> B 0x08C78BA0

The helper replays the displaced:
  SDIV W8,W20,W22

Then, for current Player-Sect NPC only, it selects a cohort using already-live native realm gap:
  gap >= 2 -> selected
  gap == 1 -> ~1/2 by month/pointer hash
  gap == 0 -> ~1/4 by month/pointer hash

For selected NPCs the helper transforms W8 so that immediately after return:

  0x0560A5F0  SUB W20,W21,W8

produces:
  W20 = 19

IMPORTANT NUANCE:
  W20 is then compared with another native dynamic value:

  0x0560A5F4 ... native getter chain
  0x0560A600 CMP  W20,W0
  0x0560A604 CSEL W21,W20,W0,GT

Therefore selected V2 does not mathematically force the final value to exactly 19 in every
possible config. It imposes an effective FLOOR of 19 at this stage:

  W21 = max(19, native_dynamic_value)

In ordinary expected configs where the native value is below 19, W21 becomes 19.

================================================================================
3. IMPORTANT CORRECTION — CommonTool.Random UPPER BOUND IS EXCLUSIVE
================================================================================

V2 notes described the following call as an "inclusive Random(19,20)" and therefore stated
that selected NPCs receive 19 or 20 opportunities.

Static cross-callsite evidence shows that interpretation is wrong.

CommonTool.Random:
  @ 0x057193C8

RunUnit call:
  0x0560A648 ADD W1,W21,#1
  0x0560A64C MOV W0,W21
  0x0560A658 BL  CommonTool.Random
  0x0560A660 STR W0,[X25,#0x20]

The strongest invariant comes from vanilla Action1004:

  0x0559FC90  LDR W21,[candidateList,#count]
  0x0559FCB4  MOV W0,#0
  0x0559FCB8  MOV W1,W21        ; max = List.Count
  0x0559FCC4  Random wrapper -> vanilla CommonTool.Random first
  0x0559FCE0  W1 = returned index
  0x0559FCE8  List.get_Item(index)

If CommonTool.Random(0,List.Count) could return List.Count, this direct index would be invalid.
Therefore the integer API is min-inclusive / max-exclusive, matching the safe list-index contract:

  [min, max)

Consequences for RunUnit:
  Random(W21, W21+1) can only return W21.

So the +1 at 0x0560A648 does NOT randomly add one extra action.

Correct V13 statement:
  selected V2 NPC receives final stored budget = W21,
  where W21 is the native value with an effective floor of 19.

Typical expected result if native value <19:
  19, not 19-or-20.

This also means the current build does NOT implement a true randomized 15-20 or 19-20 scheduler.

Confidence: HIGH.

================================================================================
4. +0x20 FIELD IS A REAL ACTION-CYCLE QUOTA, NOT A CANDIDATE COUNT
================================================================================

RunUnit allocates a generated closure/context object in X25 around 0x0560A298.
Confirmed fields used in RunUnit:
  +0x10 = WorldUnitAIBase/requester context
  +0x18 = incoming callback/context
  +0x20 = stored RunUnit budget/quota
  +0x28 = generated/list context used downstream
  +0x38/+0x40 = downstream callback/delegate state

Budget write:
  0x0560A660 STR W0,[X25,#0x20]

A downstream generated routine begins at:
  0x0560C224

It receives the same closure object in X0 -> X19.

Direct budget reads / checks:
  0x0560C5A4 LDR W9,[X19,#0x20]
  0x0560C5A8 CBZ W9,... completion path

  0x0560CDA0 LDR W8,[X19,#0x20]
  0x0560CDA4 CMP W8,#1

Direct decrement:
  0x0560CE30 LDR W8,[X19,#0x20]
  0x0560CE34 SUB W9,W8,#1
  0x0560CE3C STR W9,[X19,#0x20]

This proves +0x20 is not merely "how many candidates to build".
It survives into the downstream action scheduling/execution state and is decremented one unit at
a time after native selection/fallback reaches an actionable scheduling cycle.

The decrement occurs BEFORE the later weighted final-choice block described below.
Therefore it is best described as an action scheduling/attempt quota, not a raw candidate-list cap.

Confidence: HIGH for quota semantics; exact original managed field name remains unresolved because
this lib is stripped and the current ZIP does not include the matching metadata dump.

================================================================================
5. RUNUNIT STILL BUILDS VANILLA ELIGIBILITY ACROSS 14 TOP-LEVEL CATEGORIES
================================================================================

After the budget is stored, RunUnit clears/rebuilds native tracking collections and iterates:

  0x0560A70C MOV W24,#1
  ...
  0x0560A9EC ADD X24,X24,#1
  0x0560A9F0 CMP X24,#0xF
  0x0560A9F4 B.NE 0x0560A718

Therefore category values 1..14 are still traversed.

A candidate uniqueness/exclusion helper is:
  0x0560AB18

It forms a composite integer key:
  key = candidateID * 100 + category

Evidence:
  0x0560AB5C MOV W9,#100
  0x0560AB64 MADD W1,W19,W9,W20

and checks the AIBase tracking collection at:
  AIBase +0x58

This demonstrates that Aggressive V2 does NOT directly force Action1031, Goal101, Goal141,
Action1029, Action1004, or any specific progression action.

Vanilla category traversal, prerequisite checks, duplicate/exclusion checks, and candidate building
remain active.

Practical consequence:
  raising the quota gives the NPC more native action cycles, but progression actions must still
  survive their own eligibility and weighted-selection logic.

================================================================================
6. WEIGHTED FINAL CHOICE OCCURS AFTER ONE QUOTA UNIT IS CONSUMED
================================================================================

After the decrement at 0x0560CE3C, RunUnit reaches a native weighted chooser.

Relevant block:
  0x0560CF5C MOV W0,#0
  0x0560CF60 MOV W1,#10000
  0x0560CF6C BL  CommonTool.Random
  0x0560CF70 returned random -> W27

Then candidate entries are scanned:
  0x0560CF80 get list item
  0x0560CF88 LDR W8,[candidate,#0x80]
  0x0560CF8C CMP W27,W8
  0x0560CF90 B.LT selected/follow-up path

Because Random(0,10000) uses exclusive max, the draw is in 0..9999.

This is strong evidence that the downstream final action/goal choice is still a vanilla weighted
selection after the scheduler has increased the number of cycles.

Therefore:
  - quota increase != progression guarantee;
  - V58/V60 action-specific weights remain highly relevant;
  - an NPC can spend extra cycles on other eligible native behavior;
  - simply increasing 19 further is not the most targeted next optimization.

Confidence: HIGH for ordering and weighted chooser; exact managed candidate type/field name at +0x80
remains unresolved.

================================================================================
7. AUTOMATIC OUTER / RESERVE RECRUITMENT — TARGET SCHOOL IS NOT PREWRITTEN
================================================================================

Current automatic Outer recruitment path:
  MapBuildSchoolPostOut.AddOutUnit @ 0x05157410

Candidate iteration reaches:
  0x05157750 -> candidate WorldUnit kept in X24

Target School identity is obtained into X26, then a UnitActionSchoolJoin is allocated and initialized:

  0x05157780 MOV X1,X26
  0x05157784 MOV W2,#6          ; Outer/Initiate post type
  0x05157788 MOV W3,W22
  0x05157790 X25 = new action
  0x05157794 BL 0x05534AFC     ; UnitActionSchoolJoin.Init-like constructor

Flags set for the automatic path:
  0x051577A0 STRB #1,[X25,#0x54]
  0x051577A4 STRB #1,[X25,#0x57]

Candidate WorldUnit X24 is then passed to the action virtual method:
  0x051577A8..0x051577B4

Crucial negative result:
  AddOutUnit itself does NOT write target Player-Sect schoolID into the candidate UnitInfoData.

This agrees with the V12 binary-wide direct schoolID-write audit, which did not list AddOutUnit as
a direct persistent membership writer.

================================================================================
8. UnitActionSchoolJoin INIT -> ONCREATE — EXACT TARGET COMMIT TIMING
================================================================================

UnitActionSchoolJoin initializer:
  @ 0x05534AFC

Arguments observed from automatic recruitment:
  X1 = target School identity
  W2 = post type 6
  W3 = auxiliary join parameter

Initializer stores:
  target School identity -> action +0x30
    0x05534B8C..0x05534B98

  post type W2 -> action +0x40
    0x05534BA0

  W3 -> action +0x50
    0x05534BA4

Formal join execution:
  UnitActionSchoolJoin.OnCreate @ 0x05535EF8

Persistent membership commit:
  requester WorldUnit -> +0x68 -> +0x28 -> UnitInfoData
  target identity      <- action +0x30

  0x05536014 LDR X0,[... UnitInfoData]
  0x0553601C LDR X1,[X19,#0x30]
  0x05536028 STR X1,[X0,#0x30]!
  0x0553602C BL 0x02615928       ; generated write barrier / managed ref store support

Therefore for the automatic PostOut reserve path:

  candidate selected / queued
    -> target schoolID is only held in the UnitActionSchoolJoin object
    -> candidate is NOT made a target Player-Sect member merely by being in the reserve/candidate pool

  UnitActionSchoolJoin.OnCreate formal execution
    -> target persistent schoolID is committed
    -> later WorldUnitData.get_school can resolve/cache that target School
    -> is_player_school_npc can then pass as an official current Player-Sect member

This closes the V12 yellow question for the automatic PostOut recruitment path.

Important caveat:
  This does NOT prove every reserve candidate has an empty/no-school identity before joining.
  A candidate can have some prior native affiliation/history depending on its origin.
  What is proven is narrower and sufficient for the scope question:
    AddOutUnit does not preassign the TARGET Player-Sect schoolID before formal Join.OnCreate.

Confidence: HIGH.

================================================================================
9. IMPLICATION FOR CURRENT PLAYER-SECT SMART/CATCH-UP MODS
================================================================================

Current exact-School scope remains logically safe for automatic reserve recruitment:

Before successful formal join:
  candidate does not newly become Player-Sect merely because selected by PostOut
  -> Player-Sect-only AI/stat helpers should not activate solely from reserve selection.

After Join.OnCreate target schoolID commit and School resolution:
  official member can enter current is_player_school_npc scope
  -> V58/V60 progression helpers become eligible naturally.

No special "reserve exclusion" patch is justified from this path.

This is preferable to adding a custom reserve flag because the native membership commit already
provides the correct lifecycle boundary.

================================================================================
10. CORRECTED MODEL OF AGGRESSIVE V2
================================================================================

Old simplified note model:
  selected NPC -> Random(19,20) -> 19 or 20 goals/opportunities

Corrected V13 model:
  1) RunUnit computes native baseline/dynamic values.
  2) V2 cohort helper applies only to exact current Player-Sect NPC.
  3) selected helper makes one intermediate quantity 19.
  4) native comparison makes W21 = max(19, native dynamic value).
  5) CommonTool.Random(W21,W21+1) has an exclusive upper bound, so it returns W21.
  6) that W21 is stored at closure +0x20.
  7) native category/prerequisite/candidate generation remains intact.
  8) downstream action-cycle routine reads +0x20 and decrements it one unit at a time.
  9) final weighted native choice still occurs after quota consumption.

Thus Aggressive V2 is best described as:
  "raise the native action-cycle quota to at least 19 for the selected Player-Sect cohort"

NOT:
  "force 19-20 progression actions."

================================================================================
11. WHAT SHOULD NOT BE CHANGED YET
================================================================================

Do NOT increase the quota above 19 yet.
Reason:
  V13 now proves the quota is consumed per downstream scheduling cycle and vanilla weighted
  behavior remains active. Raising it further can increase unrelated AI work without fixing the
  actual competition among progression vs non-progression candidates.

Do NOT call get_progression_state from global RunUnit again.
Reason:
  Aggressive V1 already isolated that global progression-data dereference as an FC regression area.

Do NOT direct-write Realm/Grade.
Current native progression architecture remains preferable and already has targeted assistance in
Action1031 / Goal101 / Goal141 / material routes / exact phase-item selection.

Do NOT add a reserve-member custom scope flag.
Automatic PostOut target affiliation already begins at formal Join.OnCreate.

================================================================================

================================================================================

Highest-value next pass:

A) Trace how the V58/V60 progression weights reach the final weighted candidate threshold at +0x80.
   Goal:
     determine how much Action1031, Goal101, Goal141, Grid/material, etc. actually dominate the
     0..9999 chooser after all vanilla normalization/competition.

B) Identify the exact generated callback loop that re-enters the +0x20 consumer after each action
   attempt/completion.
   Goal:
     classify whether a failed action validation consumes one quota slot or only a launched action.

C) Map the native dynamic value compared against the V2 floor at 0x0560A600.
   Goal:
     give the +0x20 field its exact managed/config meaning and prove whether any normal config can
     naturally exceed 19.

D) If the user's intended policy remains a true randomized 15-20 budget, design it only AFTER the
   above mapping. The current Random(W21,W21+1) cannot provide that range because max is exclusive.
   A safe future implementation should be Player-Sect-scoped and should not globally alter
   CommonTool.Random semantics.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 V60
PLAYER-SECT NPC DEEP MAPPING V14
RUNUNIT ATTEMPT QUOTA / DOWNSTREAM GATE / PROGRESSION WEIGHT DATAFLOW
================================================================================
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: STATIC MAPPING ONLY — NO BINARY PATCH APPLIED
Source build: SMART_PHASE_AGGRESSIVE_CATCHUP_FCFIX_V2-1
Source lib SHA256: b115635f6ba4a4668823c6d7816c13752bf141964b4052c789ded4452be75c1f
Continuation: V13 RunUnit budget + reserve lifecycle mapping

PURPOSE
-------
V13 left three important questions:
A) Does the V58/V60 progression weight become the final candidate +0x80 value?
B) Does one RunUnit quota slot represent a successful action, or merely an attempt?
C) What exactly happens after Aggressive V2 forces the pre-RNG budget to 19?

V14 closes B, makes C exact, and substantially refines A.
It also corrects an important V13 interpretation of the downstream 0..9999 loop.

================================================================================
1. MAJOR V13 CORRECTION: 0x0560CF4C LOOP IS NOT ONE CUMULATIVE WEIGHTED DRAW
================================================================================

Relevant RunUnit tail:

  0x0560CF4C  ...
  0x0560CF5C  MOV W0,#0
  0x0560CF60  MOV W1,#10000
  0x0560CF6C  BL CommonTool.Random
  0x0560CF74  MOV W27,W0
  0x0560CF78  get candidate[index]
  0x0560CF88  LDR W8,[candidate,#0x80]
  0x0560CF8C  CMP W27,W8
  0x0560CF90  B.LT 0x0560D59C       ; candidate accepted
  0x0560CF98  index++
  0x0560CFA0  B.LT 0x0560CF4C       ; IMPORTANT: jumps before Random()

The back-edge returns to 0x0560CF4C, therefore CommonTool.Random(0,10000)
is called AGAIN for every candidate.

This is NOT:
  one random number + cumulative normalized weights.

It IS:
  an ordered sequence of independent candidate gates.

For candidate i:
  U_i = Random(0,10000) = integer 0..9999
  accept i if U_i < candidate[i].field_0x80
  first accepted candidate wins.

If p_i = threshold_i / 10000 (for a normal 0..10000 threshold), then:

  P(select candidate i)
      = p_i * product(1-p_j) for all candidates j before i

  P(no candidate passes)
      = product(1-p_i)

Therefore LIST ORDER MATTERS.
An early candidate with a high threshold can reduce the real probability that a later
progression candidate is ever reached.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

Terminology correction from V13:
- V13 called this a "final weighted chooser".
- V14 should call it a "downstream ordered independent chance gate" until the exact
  managed candidate type/semantic name is identified.

================================================================================
2. RUNUNIT +0x20 IS DEFINITIVELY AN ATTEMPT QUOTA, NOT A SUCCESSFUL-ACTION QUOTA
================================================================================

Quota check:
  0x0560CDA0  LDR W8,[X19,#0x20]
  0x0560CDA4  CMP W8,#1

Quota decrement:
  0x0560CE30  LDR W8,[X19,#0x20]
  0x0560CE34  SUB W9,W8,#1
  0x0560CE3C  STR W9,[X19,#0x20]

Downstream candidate chance loop begins later:
  0x0560CF6C  Random(0,10000)
  0x0560CF88  candidate +0x80 threshold
  0x0560CF90  branch to accepted path only if random<threshold

Thus one quota unit has ALREADY been consumed before any candidate passes +0x80.

Consequences:
- all candidates can fail their +0x80 gates;
- no candidate has to reach 0x0560D59C;
- nevertheless the RunUnit quota has already decreased by one.

So the 19-floor scheduler gives approximately 19 downstream ATTEMPTS, not 19 successful
progression actions.

This directly explains why increasing scheduler budget alone cannot guarantee realm progress.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

================================================================================
3. ACCEPTED CANDIDATE PAYLOAD: +0x88 IS PASSED TO A DOWNSTREAM DISPATCH PATH
================================================================================

Accepted path:

  0x0560D59C  accepted candidate path
  0x0560D618  obtain context
  0x0560D61C  get same candidate list item
  0x0560D634  LDR X2,[candidate,#0x88]
  0x0560D64C  BL 0x052404EC

Therefore within THIS candidate type:
- +0x80 = integer chance/gate threshold used against Random(0,10000)
- +0x88 = object/payload pointer used by downstream dispatch

Exact managed field names remain unresolved.

Confidence: HIGH.

================================================================================
4. PROGRESSION PRIORITY PATCH VALUES ARE NOT THE SAME FIELD AS RUNUNIT CANDIDATE +0x80
================================================================================

V58/V60 progression patches write to distinct locations:

A. Action1031 cultivation priority
   hook site     : 0x055AFA8C -> smart helper 0x08C78924
   final store   : 0x055AFA94 STR W8,[X19,#0x10]
   NEED_EXP only : smart helper can set W8=60000

B. Goal141 major-breakthrough seek priority
   hook site     : 0x055BAE84
   displaced op  : STR W0,[X19,#0x10]
   helper        : x20 then STR W0,[X19,#0x10]

C. Goal101 phase progression weightGrade
   hook site     : 0x055C14BC
   displaced op  : STR W0,[X19,#0x2C]
   helper        : x20 then STR W0,[X19,#0x2C]

D. Grid/material route weight
   hook site     : 0x055FD1D0
   displaced op  : STR W0,[SP,#0x4C]
   helper        : x20 then replays stack-local store

The downstream RunUnit chance field is instead:
   candidate +0x80 @ 0x0560CF88.

Therefore the patched values 60000/x20 are NOT direct writes to the downstream +0x80 gate.
There may still be an INDIRECT relationship through candidate construction/list formation,
but it is not a single shared variable and no direct dataflow from these four patch stores to
0x0560CF88 was found in this pass.

This is important: multiplying Goal/Action priority does not automatically mean the final
0..9999 gate itself becomes 100%.

Confidence:
- VERY HIGH that the storage fields are different.
- MEDIUM/HIGH that any connection is indirect, because exact candidate list builder remains unresolved.

================================================================================
5. ACTION1031 / GOAL141 +0x10 IS A REAL SHARED INTEGER PROPERTY
================================================================================

Shared getter-like routine:

  0x055B5CEC:
      CBZ X1,...
      LDR W0,[X1,#0x10]
      RET

Base/init routines nearby:
  0x055B5D00 STR WZR,[X0,#0x10]
  0x055B5D08 STR WZR,[X0,#0x10]

Action1031 stores its computed/smart priority at +0x10.
Goal141 also stores its patched native*x20 priority at +0x10.

This strongly supports +0x10 as a common integer selection/priority property for that family of
AI objects. Exact original managed property name cannot be recovered from stripped binary alone.

Confidence: HIGH.

================================================================================
6. GOAL101 HAS ADDITIONAL NATIVE RNG INSIDE ITS OWN EXECUTION REGION
================================================================================

The Goal101 region contains multiple native random branches independent of the V58 x20
weightGrade store.

Confirmed examples:

A) 0..99 branch
  0x055C177C  MOV W0,#0
  0x055C1780  MOV W1,#100
  0x055C178C  BL CommonTool.Random
  ...
  0x055C17CC  obtain config/threshold via 0x038236B4
  0x055C17D0  CMP random,threshold

B) 0..9999 branch
  0x055C21A8  obtain threshold via 0x03824BD8
  0x055C21C4  MOV W0,#0
  0x055C21C8  MOV W1,#10000
  0x055C21D4  BL CommonTool.Random
  0x055C21D8  CMP W0,W21
  0x055C21DC  B.GE alternate/fail branch

Exact semantic names of these Goal101 sub-branches remain unresolved, but they prove the
Goal101 path contains its own vanilla RNG layers after/beside its priority construction.

Meaning:
- Goal101 x20 improves priority at its mapped weightGrade site;
- it does NOT logically eliminate every later native random branch inside Goal101.

Confidence: HIGH for machine behavior; MEDIUM for gameplay interpretation of each sub-branch.

================================================================================
7. CANDIDATE-COMPATIBLE OBJECT SHOWS +0x80 CAN COME DIRECTLY FROM CONFIG DATA
================================================================================

A structurally compatible AI object constructor/setup at 0x0555EC10 shows:

  0x0555EC70  LDR X8,[X19,#0x78]
  ... list first element ...
  0x0555EC90  BL 0x076D04C0
  0x0555EC98  STR W0,[X19,#0x80]

and later:

  0x0555ECD8  LDR X8,[X20,#0x20]
  0x0555ECE4  STR X8,[X19,#0x88]

The field types line up with the RunUnit consumer:
- +0x80 integer
- +0x88 object pointer.

This is strong evidence that at least one matching AI object layout initializes +0x80 from
configuration/list data rather than from a cumulative normalization performed in RunUnit.

Caution:
The exact managed type identity linking this constructor to every object returned by the final
RunUnit list is not yet proven. Do NOT patch all +0x80 stores globally.

Confidence: MEDIUM/HIGH structural match; exact type identity YELLOW.

================================================================================
8. AGGRESSIVE V2 BUDGET FORMULA — EXACT REFINEMENT
================================================================================

Hook:
  0x0560A5E8 -> 0x08C78BA0

V2 helper begins by replaying the displaced native instruction:
  0x08C78BA0  SDIV W8,W20,W22

For selected Player-Sect NPCs it eventually executes:
  0x08C78C18  SUB W26,W21,#19
  0x08C78C1C  MOV W8,W26
  return -> 0x0560A5EC

Native continuation:
  0x0560A5F0  SUB W20,W21,W8

For selected NPC:
  W8 = W21 - 19
  therefore W20 = W21 - (W21 - 19) = 19

Then vanilla reads a configuration-derived value:
  0x0560A5F4  BL 0x032F9E70
  0x0560A5FC  BL 0x076D04C0
  0x0560A600  CMP W20,W0
  0x0560A604  CSEL W21,W20,W0,GT

So:
  pre-RNG budget = max(19, native_config_minimum)

Then:
  0x0560A648  W1=W21+1
  0x0560A64C  W0=W21
  0x0560A658  CommonTool.Random(W21,W21+1)

As established in V13, Random upper bound is exclusive, so this returns exactly W21.

Final selected quota:
  quota = max(19, native_config_minimum)

NOT:
  random 19 or 20.

If native_config_minimum <=19, quota is exactly 19.
If vanilla config minimum is ever >19, vanilla clamp wins and quota becomes >19.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

================================================================================
9. WHAT V14 CHANGES IN OUR MODEL OF NPC CATCH-UP
================================================================================

Old simplified model:
  more budget -> weighted final chooser -> progression likely.

V14 refined model:

  Player-Sect scope
      |
      v
  V2 cohort selector
      |
      v
  selected budget floor = max(19,native config minimum)
      |
      v
  native Goal/action construction across categories
      |
      +--> progression priorities (Action1031/Goal101/Goal141/Grid) affect their own fields/stages
      |
      v
  prerequisite / state checks
      |
      v
  quota--                                  <-- ATTEMPT IS CONSUMED HERE
      |
      v
  downstream candidate list
      |
      v
  candidate[0]: fresh Random(0,10000) < +0x80 ? first success wins
  candidate[1]: fresh Random(0,10000) < +0x80 ?
  candidate[2]: ...
      |
      +--> none pass: attempt can still end without accepted candidate
      |
      +--> pass: candidate +0x88 payload enters downstream dispatch

Also, individual Goal paths such as Goal101 contain additional native RNG of their own.

This is why 19 attempts + large priority multipliers can still produce occasional months where
an NPC does not advance as expected.

================================================================================
10. PATCH RECOMMENDATION AFTER V14
================================================================================

DO NOT PATCH YET.

Specifically, do NOT:
- raise scheduler quota above 19 just to hide failed attempts;- globally force candidate +0x80 to 10000;
- globally zero other candidate thresholds;
- bypass Goal101 internal RNG without identifying which sub-branch it controls.

Why:
- +0x80 is type-specific and list order matters;
- candidate semantic identity is not fully mapped;
- a global +0x80 patch could make unrelated social/world/event actions always fire;
- the current problem is now better localized to candidate construction/order and progression-specific gates.

  producer call around 0x0560CF28 -> 0x06D4F7AC.

Need to map:

The ideal future patch, if needed, is NOT "more actions".
It is likely one of:
- progression-specific candidate ordering boost;
- progression-only minimum +0x80 threshold;

while preserving all vanilla eligibility/material/realm writes.

PURPOSE
-------
Continue directly from V14.  V14 proved that:
  * RunUnit +0x20 is an ATTEMPT quota, not a successful-action quota.
  * quota is decremented before the final chance gate.
  * final chance logic uses a fresh Random(0,10000) for each candidate.
  * candidate +0x80 is a per-candidate threshold; the first candidate that passes wins.
  * accepted candidate +0x88 becomes the downstream payload.

V15 maps what happens BEFORE that final gate:
  1) how the raw candidate list is sorted,
  2) how npcGoalMaxWeightCount actually trims it,
  3) what filtering/merge stages follow,
  4) how the final +0x80 list is rebuilt from a typed registry,
  5) which parts are still not semantically named without matching global metadata.


=====================================================================================
1. IMPORTANT CORRECTION — SORT DIRECTION
=====================================================================================

A preliminary live note during this pass stated the direction backwards.
Byte-level comparator audit corrects it:

  comparer flag +0x10 == 0  => DESCENDING
  comparer flag +0x10 == 1  => ASCENDING

Proof family:
  0x06E52F84 / 0x06E5309C

Representative logic:
  FCMP left,right
  flag==0 chooses the "left > right" condition as compare-negative
  flag==1 chooses the "left < right" condition as compare-negative

In a normal comparer, negative means the left element sorts before the right element.

Therefore the actual RunUnit calls are:

  FIRST sort:
    0x0560C930  W2 = 1
    0x0560C938  BL 0x06D48F60
    => ASCENDING by selector #1 float key

  SECOND sort:
    0x0560CD48  W2 = 0
    0x0560CD50  BL 0x06D48F60
    => DESCENDING by selector #2 float key

This correction changes the interpretation of npcGoalMaxWeightCount substantially:
the first limiter keeps the HIGHEST selector #1 scores, not the lowest.


=====================================================================================
2. 0x06D48F60 — GENERIC "SELECT FLOAT KEY, THEN SORT"
=====================================================================================

Function:
  VA   0x06D48F60
  File 0x06D44F60

Machine behavior is now high-confidence:

  a) takes:
       X0 = list
       X1 = selector delegate
       W2 = direction flag
       X3 = generic MethodInfo

  b) creates a comparer-helper and stores:
       0x06D48FD0  AND  W8,W22,#1
       0x06D48FD4  STRB W8,[X23,#0x10]

  c) for every original list item:
       fetch item
       invoke X1 selector delegate
       selector returns a FLOAT in S0

  d) creates temporary pair:
       (item, float_key)

     Pair constructor 0x073951CC proves the representation:
       0x073951D4  FMOV S8,S0
       0x073951DC  STR  X1,[X0]        ; original item
       0x073951E4  STR  S8,[X19,#0x8] ; selector float key

  e) sorts the pair container through 0x071082C8 using the comparer helper

  f) copies the sorted original items back into the input list.

So this is not a shuffle and not a chance gate.
It is a deterministic sort by a delegate-produced FLOAT score/key.


=====================================================================================
3. FIRST RUNUNIT SORT — SELECTOR #1 + npcGoalMaxWeightCount
=====================================================================================

RunUnit first selector setup:
  0x0560C8C0  loads cached selector delegate from static-fields +0x10

First sort:
  0x0560C924  X0 = X21 candidate list
  0x0560C928  X1 = selector #1
  0x0560C930  W2 = 1
  0x0560C938  BL 0x06D48F60

Confirmed direction:
  ASCENDING selector #1 float key.

Then:
  0x0560C970  BL 0x032F9F78 ; npcGoalMaxWeightCount getter
  0x0560C978  conversion helper
  0x0560C99C  W1 = converted npcGoalMaxWeightCount
  0x0560C9A4  W2 = 0
  0x0560C9AC  BL 0x06C82BBC

Thus the limiter receives:
  list = X21
  K    = npcGoalMaxWeightCount
  offset = 0


=====================================================================================
4. 0x06C82BBC — npcGoalMaxWeightCount IS A FRONT-TRIM LIMITER
=====================================================================================

Function:
  VA   0x06C82BBC
  File 0x06C7EBBC

With RunUnit's W2=0:

  Count = list.Count

  if Count <= K:
      leave list unchanged

  else:
      remove_count = Count - K

      repeat remove_count times:
          RemoveAt(0)

Exact loop anchor:
  0x06C82CE0  SUB W21,W0,W21  ; Count-K
  ...
  0x06C82D64  MOV W1,WZR      ; removal index = 0
  0x06C82D68  BLR X8          ; virtual remove-at-index
  ...
  loop until removal_count exhausted

Because the list was first sorted ASCENDING, repeatedly deleting index 0 removes
the LOWEST selector #1 keys.

RESULT:
  npcGoalMaxWeightCount keeps the HIGHEST K selector #1 candidates.

This resolves the old V13/V14 uncertainty around this parameter.
It is not random and it is not the monthly attempt budget.
It is a preselection TOP-K limiter operating on a float selector score.


=====================================================================================
5. CRITICAL CAUTION — SELECTOR #1 IS NOT YET PROVEN TO BE OUR PATCHED "WEIGHT"
=====================================================================================

We can now prove:
  * selector #1 returns float;
  * first list is ascending by that float;
  * TOP-K largest float keys survive.

We CANNOT yet prove from the supplied stripped build that selector #1 directly reads:
  Action1031 +0x10,
  Goal141    +0x10,
  Goal101    +0x2C,
  Grid       SP+0x4C,
or any one of the previously patched integer priority fields.

The selector may:
  * directly convert a weight,
  * calculate a derived score,
  * invert/normalize a value,
  * use another candidate property,
  * combine several values.

Therefore DO NOT reverse multipliers or patch npcGoalMaxWeightCount merely from the
word "weight".  The machine-level TOP-K behavior is green; exact managed selector
semantics remain yellow.


=====================================================================================
6. POST-TRIM PIPELINE — APPEND, MERGE, ELIGIBILITY REMOVAL
=====================================================================================

After the TOP-K trim, RunUnit does not jump directly to the +0x80 chance gate.

Observed stages:

A) Extra selection/append
  0x0560CA50  BL 0x06C844A4
  A non-null returned object can be added to the working list.

B) Build another requester-linked eligible list
  RunUnit enumerates a requester-side collection reachable through +0x68.
  Each item is evaluated through:
      0x0543E6A8(requester, item+0x10, 1, ...)
  Returned object's byte +0x38 is tested before adding.

C) Merge into working list
  0x0560CBF4  BL 0x0716793C
  Structure is AddRange-like, but exact managed method name is left unresolved.

D) Explicit candidate-removal pass
  0x0560CC38  BL 0x0560ABB8(requester,candidate)
  if true:
      0x0560CC4C removes current index from X20

This is a real eligibility/removal layer.  A candidate with a strong upstream score
can still disappear here.


=====================================================================================
7. TYPE-REGISTRY PRESENCE PROBE BEFORE SECOND SORT
=====================================================================================

RunUnit then asks for a typed collection:

  requester chain:
    [RunUnit +0x10] -> object
    object +0x10    -> source passed as X0

  0x0560CC88  BL 0x06D4F7AC

It reads the resulting list count:
  0x0560CC90  LDR W8,[X0,#0x18]

Then:
  0x0560CCA4  stores (count > 0) into capture object +0x10
  if count >= 1:
  0x0560CCBC  sets owner/requester byte +0x60 = 1

Capture layout resolved at this site:
  capture +0x10 = boolean derived from typed-list presence
  capture +0x18 = RunUnit/context reference (stored earlier in the function)

This capture later participates in a predicate closure.


=====================================================================================
8. SECOND SORT — DISTINCT SELECTOR #2, DESCENDING
=====================================================================================

A second cached selector delegate is stored at static-fields +0x20.

Call:
  0x0560CD3C  X0 = X20 working list
  0x0560CD40  X1 = selector #2
  0x0560CD48  W2 = 0
  0x0560CD50  BL 0x06D48F60

Using the corrected comparator direction:
  W2=0 => DESCENDING.

So the surviving/merged working list is now reordered from HIGHEST to LOWEST
selector #2 float key.

Important:
  selector #2 is a distinct delegate from selector #1.
  Its exact managed formula remains unresolved without matching metadata.


=====================================================================================
9. 0x07168B88 — POST-SORT PREDICATE REMOVAL IS List.RemoveAll-LIKE
=====================================================================================

After the second descending sort:

  0x0560CD64..0x0560CD7C creates a predicate delegate capturing X23
  0x0560CD94 calls 0x07168B88(X20,predicate)

Static audit of 0x07168B88 proves a classic in-place predicate removal pattern:
  * predicate must be non-null,
  * scan until first matching element,
  * scan remaining elements,
  * compact non-matching elements downward,
  * clear trailing references,
  * update list count/version,
  * return number removed.

Therefore this is a List.RemoveAll-style filter.
It preserves the relative order of elements that survive.

Likely closure body:
  0x0560DA24

That function uses:
  capture +0x10,
  candidate +0x18 -> +0x28,
  candidate +0x2C,
  and conditionally calls a candidate virtual method around vtable +0x1B8.

The body shape fits the captured predicate extremely well, but without MethodInfo/global
metadata linkage this exact delegate-to-function association remains YELLOW rather than GREEN.


=====================================================================================
10. ATTEMPT QUOTA STILL DECREMENTS BEFORE FINAL CHANCE LIST
=====================================================================================

V14 result remains valid.

After the second sort/filter stage:
  0x0560CDA0  read attempt quota [RunUnit+0x20]
  ...
  0x0560CE3C  write decremented quota

Thus one attempt can still be consumed before a final +0x80 candidate actually passes.


=====================================================================================
11. 0x06D4F7AC — FINAL LIST HELPER IS A TYPE-REGISTRY COLLECTOR, NOT A SORTER
=====================================================================================

Function:
  VA   0x06D4F7AC
  File 0x06D4B7AC

V15 fully audits this helper.

Core anchor:
  0x06D4F85C
      LDR X21,[X21,#0xC0]

It then:
  * performs a generic/type-key lookup against the source registry/collection,
  * obtains the matching enumerable,
  * creates a fresh list,
  * enumerates matching members,
  * appends them one-by-one to the fresh list,
  * returns that list.

There is NO sort, shuffle, Random, or normalization inside 0x06D4F7AC.

Therefore:
  order returned by this helper follows the enumeration order of the matched source collection.

Caution:
  this does not prove "creation order globally"; upstream registry insertion/maintenance can
  already determine that source order.


=====================================================================================
12. FINAL +0x80 LIST IS NOT X20 DIRECTLY
=====================================================================================

This distinction is important.

After sorting/filtering X20, RunUnit calls:
  0x0560CEE8  BL 0x06C844A4(X20,...)

The selected result is stored in a wrapper-like object at +0x10.

Then, separately:

  0x0560CF0C  [RunUnit+0x10]
  0x0560CF14  load source object +0x10 as X0
  0x0560CF28  BL 0x06D4F7AC

This returns the list X24 that is actually iterated by the final chance gate.

So the correct structural pipeline is NOT:
  X20 -> immediately read +0x80.

Instead it is approximately:

  raw candidate list
      |
      v
  selector #1 float sort ASC
      |
      v
  npcGoalMaxWeightCount TOP-K (delete low scores)
      |
      v
  append / requester-linked merge
      |
      v
  0x0560ABB8 eligibility removal
      |
      v
  typed-registry presence probe
      |
      v
  selector #2 float sort DESC
      |
      v
  RemoveAll predicate
      |
      v
  attempt quota stage
      |
      v
  0x06C844A4 selection / wrapper state
      |
      v
  fresh typed-registry extraction 0x06D4F7AC
      |
      v
  ordered per-candidate +0x80 Random(0,10000) gates
      |
      v
  first pass -> candidate +0x88 -> downstream action dispatch


=====================================================================================
13. FINAL GATE ORDER — V14 RESULT NOW HAS A FIRMER SOURCE
=====================================================================================

At:
  0x0560CF28  final typed list is obtained
  0x0560CF30  count
  0x0560CF4C  loop start
  0x0560CF6C  Random(0,10000)
  0x0560CF80  get list[index]
  0x0560CF88  read candidate +0x80
  0x0560CF90  if random < +0x80 -> accept
  otherwise increment index and repeat with a NEW random draw.

0x06D4F7AC does not reorder that typed source list.
Thus the source registry's enumeration order is operationally important:
an earlier high-threshold candidate can prevent later candidates from ever being tested
on that attempt once it passes.


=====================================================================================
14. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR OUR CURRENT SMART NPC PATCH
=====================================================================================

V15 changes the diagnosis in an important way:

1) npcGoalMaxWeightCount is now understood mechanically.
   It keeps TOP-K candidates by selector #1 score after ascending sort/front-trim.

2) We should NOT increase it blindly.
   A larger K increases candidate diversity but may also let more low-score/native actions
   survive into later filtering and cost more monthly processing.

3) We should NOT change the current ×20 / 60000 priorities yet.
   We still need the exact selector #1 and selector #2 formulas before claiming how those
   integer priorities map to these float scores.

4) The final +0x80 bottleneck is a separate layer.
   A candidate can:
      survive TOP-K,
      survive merge/filter,
      survive second sort/RemoveAll,
   and still fail its independent final +0x80 chance.

5) The final +0x80 list is extracted from a typed registry, not directly from the X20
   intermediate candidate list.  So patching X20 order alone may not guarantee the final
   gate order.

6) Current recommendation remains MAPPING ONLY.
   No safe reason yet to raise action quota above the existing floor 19.


=====================================================================================

   Goal:

   Goal:
     find a safe progression-only threshold patch, if one is needed, rather than a
     global chance change.

F) Link likely predicate 0x0560DA24 formally through MethodInfo/delegate metadata.
   Matching global-metadata.dat for this exact build would make this much easier.

TALE OF IMMORTAL ANDROID ARM64 — V60 PLAYER-SECT NPC DEEP MAPPING V16
EFFECTIVE CANDIDATE SCORE / WEIGHTED-RANDOM SELECTOR / PROGRESSION WEIGHT DATAFLOW
=====================================================================================
Date: 2026-09-01
Status: STATIC MAPPING ONLY — NO BINARY PATCH APPLIED
Source build: Tale_of_Immortal_ARM64_v60_SMART_PHASE_AGGRESSIVE_CATCHUP_FCFIX_V2-1
Source lib SHA256: b115635f6ba4a4668823c6d7816c13752bf141964b4052c789ded4452be75c1f
Architecture: AArch64 Android IL2CPP
Continuation: V15 PRESELECTION SORT/TRIM PIPELINE

PURPOSE
-------
V15 proved the structural pipeline but left the two float selector formulas and
0x06C844A4 unresolved. V16 focuses on four questions:

  A) What are selector #1 and selector #2 actually reading?
  B) What does 0x06C844A4 do at the two selection sites?
  C) Do our existing Goal101 / Action1031 / Goal141 weights really reach a native
     weighted selection layer?
  D) What exactly does the post-sort RemoveAll predicate remove?

V16 answers A-D with high/very-high confidence and corrects one terminology point:
the third argument passed as X2=0 into the weighted helper is a NULL RNG/provider
context, NOT an integer "mode 0" selector.

=====================================================================================
1. FORMAL METHODDEF -> METHOD-POINTER LINKAGE FOR THE RUNUNIT SELECTOR FAMILY
=====================================================================================

The stripped lib still contains encoded MethodDef usage values behind the cached
delegate holders. These can be linked to the generated method-pointer relocation
array by relative slot order.

Relevant holder relocations:

  holder VA 0x08172458 -> usage data 0x08568370 = 0x6002AE5F
  holder VA 0x08172460 -> usage data 0x08568378 = 0x6002AE61
  holder VA 0x08172468 -> usage data 0x08568380 = 0x6002AE63
  holder VA 0x08172448 -> usage data 0x08568360 = 0x6002AE65
  holder VA 0x08172450 -> usage data 0x08568368 = 0x6002AE67
  holder VA 0x08172470 -> usage data 0x085683C8 = 0x6002AE85

Generated method-pointer relocation slots:

  0x084A9860 -> 0x0560BA88
  0x084A9868 -> 0x0560BA9C
  0x084A9870 -> 0x0560BAC8
  0x084A9878 -> 0x0560BAF0
  0x084A9880 -> 0x0560BB1C
  ...
  0x084A98F8 -> 0x0560DA24

The sequence is internally exact:

  2AE5F -> 0x0560BA88
  2AE61 -> 0x0560BA9C
  2AE63 -> 0x0560BAC8
  2AE65 -> 0x0560BAF0
  2AE67 -> 0x0560BB1C

For the later predicate:
  encoded usage difference 0x2AE85 - 0x2AE67 = 0x1E
  tagged MethodDef step = 2 per method
  -> 15 method slots later

Pointer relocation difference:
  0x084A98F8 - 0x084A9880 = 0x78
  0x78 / 8 bytes = 15 slots

Therefore:
  0x6002AE85 -> 0x0560DA24

This closes the V15 "likely predicate" linkage and also identifies the selector
bodies without requiring the matching global-metadata.dat.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
2. SELECTOR #1 AND SELECTOR #2 — EXACT EFFECTIVE SCORE FORMULA
=====================================================================================

Selector #1 float body:
  0x0560BA9C

Core:
  candidate = X1
  nested    = [candidate + 0x18]
  base      = [nested + 0x28]      (int32)
  dynamic   = [candidate + 0x2C]   (int32)
  score     = base + dynamic
  return float(score)

Disassembly anchors:
  0x0560BAA4  LDR X8,[X1,#0x18]
  0x0560BAAC  LDR W8,[X8,#0x28]
  0x0560BAB0  LDR W9,[X1,#0x2C]
  0x0560BAB4  ADD W8,W9,W8
  0x0560BAB8  SCVTF S0,W8

Selector #1 integer twin:
  0x0560BAC8

It returns exactly the same sum as W0:
  score = [candidate+0x2C] + [[candidate+0x18]+0x28]

Selector #2 float body:
  0x0560BAF0

Selector #2 integer twin:
  0x0560BB1C

Both repeat THE SAME formula.

Therefore V15's two "distinct selector delegates" are distinct delegate/method
instances but NOT distinct scoring formulas. They compute the same effective score.

Canonical V16 structural name:

  EFFECTIVE CANDIDATE SCORE
    = base_component     ([[candidate+0x18]+0x28])
    + dynamic_component ([candidate+0x2C])

Exact original managed field names remain unavailable in the stripped build, so
"base_component" and "dynamic_component" are structural names only.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
3. SAME SCORE DRIVES FIRST SORT, TOP-K, AND FIRST WEIGHTED PICK
=====================================================================================

From V15:
  0x0560C938 -> 0x06D48F60 with W2=1
  => ASCENDING by selector #1 float score.

Then:
  npcGoalMaxWeightCount @ 0x032F9F78
  0x0560C9AC -> 0x06C82BBC

The limiter removes index 0 until Count<=K.
Because the list is ascending, the LOWEST scores are removed.
Thus the HIGHEST K effective scores survive.

V16 now adds the next stage:
  0x0560CA50 -> 0x06C844A4

The cached delegate at this site resolves to:
  0x0560BAC8

So after TOP-K, the selection helper receives the SAME effective score as an integer.
It is not a separate random metric.

Pipeline so far:

  raw candidates
    -> effective_score = base + dynamic
    -> ASC sort
    -> keep highest K via npcGoalMaxWeightCount
    -> cumulative weighted-random pick using effective_score

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
4. 0x06C844A4 — SELECT ONE ELEMENT BY AN INTEGER WEIGHT DELEGATE
=====================================================================================

Function:
  VA   0x06C844A4
  File 0x06C804A4

RunUnit sites:
  first  0x0560CA50
  second 0x0560CEE8

Behavior:

  if source is null/empty:
      return null

  index = 0x06C85208(source, integer_weight_delegate, rng_context)

  if index == -1:
      return null

  return source[index]

Key anchors:
  0x06C84578..0x06C8458C -> call 0x06C85208
  0x06C84590              -> compare returned index with -1
  0x06C84598..0x06C84630 -> source indexer with selected index

Thus 0x06C844A4 is a SELECT-ONE helper whose index is obtained from an integer
weight function. Exact managed method name remains unresolved.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
5. 0x06C85208 — MATERIALIZES THE INTEGER WEIGHT ARRAY
=====================================================================================

0x06C85208 iterates every source item:

  for i in 0..Count-1:
      item = source[i]
      weight[i] = delegate(item)    ; W0 integer

It creates an int[]/equivalent integer array containing those delegate results, then
passes the array to:

  0x0571A8BC(weights, rng_context)

Therefore the RunUnit selector twins at 0x0560BAC8 / 0x0560BB1C are not merely
sort keys. Their integer result becomes the actual weighted-random mass.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
6. 0x0571A8BC — EXACT CUMULATIVE POSITIVE-WEIGHT RANDOM INDEX
=====================================================================================

The weighted-index helper is now mapped sufficiently to state its probability model.

A) Build intervals @ 0x0571ABA0
--------------------------------
For every weight i:

  lower[i] = cumulative

  if weight[i] >= 1:
      cumulative += weight[i]

  upper[i] = cumulative

Anchor:
  0x0571AC40  store current cumulative to lower[]
  0x0571AC44  load original weight
  0x0571AC4C  compare weight with 1
  0x0571AC54  cumulative += weight when positive
  0x0571AC5C  store cumulative to upper[]

Non-positive weights therefore contribute ZERO selectable mass.

B) Random draw
--------------
Total positive mass is held in the cumulative field.
The helper calls:

  CommonTool.Random(1, total+1, rng_context)

Anchors:
  0x0571AB2C  W1 = total+1
  0x0571AB30  W0 = 1
  0x0571AB34  X2 = caller-provided RNG context
  0x0571AB38  BL 0x057193C8

From V13, integer Random uses an EXCLUSIVE upper bound, therefore this draw is:

  1 .. total inclusive

C) Resolve index @ 0x0571AC80
-----------------------------
The helper scans candidates and accepts the first positive-weight interval containing
the draw. It initializes result to -1 and writes the matching index when found.

Thus for positive weights:

  P(select i) = weight[i] / sum(all positive weights)

If all weights are <=0, no selectable positive mass exists and the selection can
return -1 / null through the upstream helper.

IMPORTANT V16 CORRECTION:
The X2 argument passed as zero by RunUnit is not an integer "mode 0". In
CommonTool.Random @ 0x057193C8, X2 is treated as an RNG/provider context. If null,
the routine obtains the default RNG instance. So RunUnit uses the normal/default RNG.

Confidence: VERY HIGH for the weighted probability mechanics.
Confidence: HIGH for the RNG-context interpretation.

=====================================================================================
7. SECOND SORT + SECOND WEIGHTED PICK USE THE SAME EFFECTIVE SCORE AGAIN
=====================================================================================

Second sort:
  0x0560CD50 -> 0x06D48F60 with W2=0
  => DESCENDING

Delegate body:
  0x0560BAF0
  => float(base + dynamic)

After RemoveAll and the attempt-quota decrement, the second select-one call is:
  0x0560CEE8 -> 0x06C844A4

Its integer delegate body:
  0x0560BB1C
  => int(base + dynamic)

So the same score governs:

  1) first ASC sort
  2) TOP-K retention
  3) first cumulative weighted pick
  4) second DESC sort
  5) zero-weight predicate decision (next section)
  6) second cumulative weighted pick

This is the central V16 finding.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
8. 0x0560DA24 — FORMALLY LINKED ZERO-WEIGHT + ELIGIBILITY PREDICATE
=====================================================================================

V15 only called this the likely RemoveAll predicate.
V16's MethodDef/relocation linkage proves it is the cached predicate body used by the
post-sort filter family.

Core:
  nested  = [candidate+0x18]
  base    = [nested+0x28]
  dynamic = [candidate+0x2C]

  0x0560DA3C  CMN W9,W8
  0x0560DA40  B.EQ remove

CMN tests (dynamic + base) == 0.
So:

  if effective_score == 0:
      predicate returns true -> RemoveAll removes candidate

If effective_score !=0:
  - if capture flag +0x10 is false, candidate is kept;
  - if capture flag is true, candidate virtual method around vtable +0x1B8 is called;
  - if that virtual eligibility result is false, predicate returns true -> remove;
  - otherwise keep.

No RNG exists in this predicate.

This also independently confirms that base+dynamic is a meaningful selection weight,
not merely a display/order value.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================9. GOAL101 +0x2C PATCH NOW HAS A DIRECT PATH INTO THE EFFECTIVE SCORE
=====================================================================================

Current Goal101 patch site:
  0x055C14BC -> custom x20 helper
  native/replayed result is stored at Goal101 object +0x2C

V14 had to treat the connection from Goal101 +0x2C to RunUnit selection as indirect
and unresolved.

V16 identifies candidate dynamic_component at exactly:
  candidate +0x2C

Therefore, whenever a Goal101 instance is one of the candidate entries processed by
this selector family, its patched +0x2C value is DIRECTLY one addend of the effective
candidate score:

  effective Goal101 candidate score
      = base_component + patched_dynamic_component

With the current x20 helper, this should be understood as boosting the dynamic
component, not blindly multiplying every later field such as +0x80.

This is exactly why Goal101 can dominate TOP-K / weighted candidate selection while
still retaining later native RNG and the final +0x80 action gate.

Confidence: VERY HIGH for +0x2C being the selector's dynamic field.
Confidence: HIGH for the Goal101 candidate dataflow qualification above.

=====================================================================================
10. ACTION1031 / GOAL141 +0x10 — DIRECT NATIVE WEIGHTED-SELECT CONSUMER FOUND
=====================================================================================

Shared +0x10 getter:
  0x055B5CEC
      LDR W0,[X1,#0x10]
      RET

V14 knew this was a common priority property but had not found its consumer.
V16 maps it to a native cumulative weighted-selection site.

Method-pointer relocation:
  0x084A8228 -> 0x055B5CEC

Using the same relative MethodDef mapping anchored by the V16 RunUnit family, the
encoded MethodDef usage for this getter is:
  0x6002A8D1

Its single encoded usage datum is located at:
  data VA 0x08568230

Holder relocation:
  0x081705C8 -> 0x08568230

Consumer path:
  0x055B4EE4 loads holder 0x081705C8
  0x055B4EF0 builds the delegate
  0x055B4F38 calls 0x06C844A4(list, +0x10 getter delegate, null RNG context)

Therefore the common +0x10 property is DIRECTLY used as integer weight mass by the
same cumulative weighted-random selection architecture mapped in sections 4-6.

Consequences for current mods:

  Action1031 NEED_EXP smart value:
      object +0x10 = 60000
      -> directly raises this local weighted-selection probability

  Goal141 x20 value:
      object +0x10 = patched result
      -> directly raises this local weighted-selection probability

These values are NOT dead/disconnected weights.
They are real native weighted-selection inputs at a DIFFERENT layer from the
outer candidate base+dynamic score.

Confidence: VERY HIGH.

=====================================================================================
11. TWO DISTINCT SELECTION LAYERS — UPDATED MODEL
=====================================================================================

V16 can now separate two previously mixed concepts.

LAYER A — OUTER CANDIDATE EFFECTIVE SCORE
----------------------------------------
  effective_score = [[candidate+0x18]+0x28] + [candidate+0x2C]

Used by:
  - TOP-K ranking
  - first weighted pick
  - second ordering
  - zero-score removal
  - second weighted pick

Goal101 +0x2C directly contributes here when Goal101 is present as the relevant
candidate entry.

LAYER B — INTERNAL +0x10 WEIGHT FAMILY
--------------------------------------
  getter 0x055B5CEC -> [object+0x10]
  direct 0x06C844A4 cumulative weighted select @ 0x055B4F38

Action1031 and Goal141 patched +0x10 values directly contribute here.

LAYER C — FINAL TYPED ACTION CHANCE GATE
----------------------------------------
From V14/V15:
  candidate +0x80 vs fresh Random(0,10000) for each final typed entry
  first passing entry wins
  +0x88 payload dispatched

This layer is still separate.

Therefore a strong progression weight can win Layers A/B and STILL fail or be delayed
by later eligibility / final typed-list +0x80 chance / goal-specific internal RNG.

This is a much better explanation for runtime inconsistency than simply saying
"19 actions are not enough."

=====================================================================================
12. GRID/MATERIAL x20 — CURRENT V16 STATUS
=====================================================================================

Grid/material route patch remains structurally different:
  hook 0x055FD1D0
  patched result is replayed to stack local SP+0x4C

The surrounding code then converts/uses that integer through:
  0x076D0190
  0x0755DC8C
and inserts the resulting object into a route/list.

This proves the patched value is operational in the route-building path, but V16 does
NOT yet prove that SP+0x4C is one of the same outer candidate fields mapped above.
Do not label it candidate+0x2C or +0x10 without further tracing.

Confidence: HIGH that the x20 affects the route-building decision.
Confidence: YELLOW for equivalence to either mapped candidate-weight field.

=====================================================================================
13. IMPLICATION FOR npcGoalMaxWeightCount
=====================================================================================

V15 already proved npcGoalMaxWeightCount keeps the highest K candidates.
V16 now proves which score is being ranked:

  K highest of:
      [[candidate+0x18]+0x28] + [candidate+0x2C]

Therefore increasing npcGoalMaxWeightCount has a very specific effect:
  - it allows lower-effective-score candidates to survive preselection;
  - it does NOT strengthen Action1031/Goal141 +0x10 directly;
  - it can increase candidate diversity but can also reintroduce unrelated low-score goals;
  - it is still not the same thing as the 19 attempt quota.

This reinforces the recommendation to leave npcGoalMaxWeightCount vanilla until
runtime evidence shows progression candidates are being cut by TOP-K.

=====================================================================================
14. PATCH RECOMMENDATION AFTER V16
=====================================================================================

NO NEW PATCH RECOMMENDED YET.

Do NOT raise action quota above 19 merely because some NPCs still miss progression.
Do NOT globally change npcGoalMaxWeightCount.
Do NOT globally force +0x80 to 10000.

Why:
  - Action1031 60000 is now proven to be a real local weighted-selection input;
  - Goal141 x20 is likewise a real local weighted-selection input;
  - Goal101 +0x2C is directly part of outer effective-score selection;
  - the current remaining uncertainty has moved further downstream/upstream:
      * exact typed +0x80 progression entries and their config writers;
      * exact source-registry +0xC0 producer/insertion order;
      * Grid/material stack-local -> final route/candidate bridge;
      * classification of Goal101 internal RNG branches.

The current mod is already pushing the intended progression weights at real native
selection points. Further multiplying them blindly can create extreme dominance with
little benefit if the remaining failures happen after selection.

Exact matching V60 global-metadata.dat recovered from the user's Library:
